F350 | api-places-enrichment | places-promotion.service.ts:383-398,539-551 + tomtom-chain-probe.adapter.ts:354-360,602-607 vs governance.service.ts:721-726 | "A CONSUMED DRAW" IS DEFINED THREE DIFFERENT WAYS IN ONE FLOW, AND THE TWO THAT COST MONEY DISAGREE WITH THE ONE THAT SPENDS IT. governance.drawWithOutcome debits the pool on a THROW ("we conservatively debit 1 — the request likely reached the vendor"). The adapter's `recordDraw()` (api_usage_ledger) runs ONLY after a non-null response, so a transport error writes no ledger row. The promotion service's `draws.scarce` (the CAMPAIGN ENVELOPE) is likewise never incremented on the transport-error path — while promoteOne's own docstring states it meters "promoted, consumed-draw miss, wrong-entity reject, no-rings, TRANSPORT ERROR", and the catch block's comment asserts "the draw was conservatively debited". Both claims are false of the code beside them. Consequence: on a TomTom fault the rate pool is debited, the campaign envelope is not, and cost-reconcile cannot see the draw at all — the same "summed the wrong column" class as the $118 Places lesson, one level down, and the same class as the campaign envelope that "was sized for Places spend it never debited" (round-six cost #4). | LADDER: compensates for the draw being counted at three levels that were each built at a different time, with no shared definition. Bedrock: a vendor draw is ONE event; who was charged is a property of the draw, not of the caller that noticed it. Rederive: `governance.drawWithOutcome` is the only place that knows whether the vendor was reached, so it should RETURN that fact — `{admitted, value, drawsConsumed}` — and both the ledger write and the campaign meter should hang off that single return rather than off each caller's guess. The adapter's recordDraw and the promotion service's `draws` counter then stop being independent re-derivations of a fact the governor already computed. Red team: makes under-metering unrepresentable rather than merely untested; costs a signature change at ~8 governed call sites; the new failure is OVER-metering when a request genuinely never left the process (connect-refused), which is the conservative direction the pool already chose deliberately. | FIXED-PARTIAL (D29a accounting-truth half landed 2026-08-03; the metering GAP itself stays ESCALATED) | PROOF(pass1): read all three; governance.service.ts:721-726 debits in `catch`, adapter recordDraw is after the null-check, promotion `draws.scarce += 1` appears only at :558 and :573, never in the :539 catch. || PASS-3 PROOF (D29a): the two draw words are DEFINED ONCE, in governance.drawWithOutcome's docstring — ADMITTED (pool, debits on throw) vs ANSWERED (ledger + campaign, hang off a non-null response) — and all three meters now state which they count. promoteOne's docstring no longer lists 'transport error' among metered exits; the :539 catch no longer claims a debit the ledger/campaign never made; the adapter's recordDraw says ANSWERED. Spend behaviour byte-unchanged (no call site added, removed or reordered); yarn build + full suite green.

F351 | api-places-enrichment | apps/api/src/modules/places/places-containment.integration.spec.ts:407,433 | THE ONLY DB-BACKED TEST IN THE REPO DID NOT COMPILE, AND BOTH MECHANISMS THAT SHOULD HAVE CAUGHT IT WERE DARK. This is the file whose own header says a law enforced by the database has to be proven against the database, and that it "FAILS LOUDLY without [DATABASE_URL] rather than skipping: a silently-skipped test proves nothing, which is the exact disease this file treats". It was not skipping — it was failing to compile (`new SignalDemandAggregateService(prisma, logger)` against a constructor that gained an `opsAlerts` parameter, almost certainly from the concurrent api-signals pass landing F205). It is excluded from `yarn test` by testPathIgnorePatterns, so only `yarn test:db` runs it, and only CI runs `yarn test:db` — and CI dies at the `Lint` step, which precedes both `Type check` and `test:db`, on every one of the last 6 runs. So the twelve containment laws — including the largest-gap antimeridian law that this very file proved RED twice before its fix — have been unguarded. EXECUTED: `npx tsc --noEmit` reports these two lines as the ONLY type errors in the entire API. | LADDER: compensates for nothing; it is rot admitted by a gate nobody watches. Bedrock: a guard that cannot run is not a guard, and "excluded from the default test run" plus "the only lane that runs it is behind an earlier failing step" is two independent single points of failure on the repo's most expensive-to-rebuild knowledge. Rederive: (a) run the integration lane BEFORE lint in CI, or at minimum make lint non-blocking for the test steps, so a green-tests signal cannot be hidden behind a formatting failure; (b) since `yarn type-check` DOES catch this class in one second, make type-check the first CI step; (c) better still, delete the testPathIgnorePatterns exclusion and have the spec skip-with-failure on a missing DATABASE_URL exactly as its header already promises, so `yarn test` locally surfaces the breakage the moment it happens. Red team: (a)+(b) are pure ordering with no downside; (c) makes every local `yarn test` need a database, which is a real cost — so (b) is the one I would land first, and it is free. | FIXED (test-only; production untouched) — added the missing OpsAlertsService stub argument at both call sites. PROOF: `npx jest --testPathIgnorePatterns='[]' --testRegex='places-containment.*integration'` against the local mirror now runs GREEN, 12/12, including "THE ALEUTIAN CLASS", "the UK class", "P4 DERIVED BBOX AT THE SEAM" and "THE BBOX LIES, THE GROUND JUDGES". Before the fix: "Test suite failed to run", 0 tests. The CI-ordering half remains OWNER-DECISION.

F352 | api-places-enrichment | restaurant-secondary-location-expansion-queue.service.ts:37-51 + restaurant-secondary-location-expansion.worker.ts:49 + restaurant-location-enrichment.service.ts:716-722 | THE ONE ENRICHMENT LANE THAT SPENDS THE MOST PLACES MONEY IS THE ONE LANE WITH NO CAMPAIGN ENVELOPE. Three separate failures compound on the same path: (a) the expansion queue does NOT capture `currentCampaignId()` into its payload and its job type has no campaignId field, and the worker does NOT `runInWorkContext` — so every Places call this lane makes escapes every campaign budget. The sibling primary-enrichment queue captures it and documents exactly why ("AsyncLocalStorage does not cross the BullMQ boundary (round-six cost #4: the envelope was sized for Places spend it never debited)") — the lesson was learned in one queue and never carried to the other two. (b) The worker's entry point calls `getPlaceDetails(placeId, { includeRaw: true })` — the FULL mask — while labelling the work `attribution: 'grounding.refresh'`; `refreshStaleLocations` was deliberately built to use the lean REFRESH_PLACE_DETAILS_FIELD_MASK_FIELDS precisely so refresh polls "bill below the Enterprise+Atmosphere SKU the full mask forces". So the attribution says refresh and the invoice says enterprise+atmosphere, and the regrounding rate is understated per call. (c) The lane then paginates `findPlaceFromText` with a 2s delay and no campaign check between pages. | LADDER: (a) compensates for the campaign being ambient rather than carried; bedrock: a queued job is a detached unit of work, so everything it needs to be budgeted must be IN the payload — which is why one queue already does it, and the fix is to make that structural rather than remembered (a shared `enqueueWithWorkContext` helper, or a payload base type carrying campaignId that the worker's runner re-establishes for every queue). (b) compensates for two SKUs and one method; bedrock: the field mask determines the price, so "which mask" is not a call-site option, it is the identity of the operation — `getPlaceDetailsRefresh()` and `getPlaceDetailsFull()` as separate methods make a refresh that bills enterprise impossible to write. Red team: (a) touches 3 queues and 3 workers, no data migration, and closes a live budget hole; (b) is a mechanical split whose only new failure is a caller genuinely needing the full mask on a refresh path, which then has to say so out loud. | FIXED-PARTIAL (D29a label half landed 2026-08-03; (a) campaign capture and the (b) MASK SPLIT stay ESCALATED) | PROOF(pass1): read; grep confirms `currentCampaignId` appears in restaurant-enrichment-queue.service.ts and in NEITHER sibling queue; `runInWorkContext` appears in restaurant-enrichment.worker.ts and in NEITHER sibling worker. || PASS-3 PROOF (D29a): the expansion lane's ledger attribution is 'grounding.expansion', not 'grounding.refresh' — it bills the FULL mask (Enterprise+Atmosphere) and the label now says which lane it is. spend-analytics excludes it from BOTH published rates with a stated reason (two SKUs in one bucket produce a per-refresh unit cost true of neither). RED-proved: relabelling the spec fixture back to 'grounding.refresh' fails 2 spend-analytics cases. The CALL is byte-identical — mask unchanged, no campaign capture. || PASS-3 PROOF (F352a, owner-ruled 2026-08-03): (a) IS NOW FIXED. `currentCampaignId()` is captured into the expansion job payload and `runInWorkContext` re-established in the worker — the same mechanism the primary-enrichment and attribute-ontology lanes use, and the bulk paths that enqueue this work (city-reextract.runner:91, gemini-batch.service:676) already establish that context, so the capture is live rather than theoretical. THE RULING BOUNDS IT TO ATTRIBUTION: routine keyword/chronological collection carries no campaign, so the field is absent and the lane behaves exactly as before — pool-governed and APPROVAL-FREE FOREVER; campaigns are for one-time bulk events only. Verified there is no gate: usage-ledger's meterCampaignSpend is post-hoc, fire-and-forget metering (:299) with no refusal path, so capture adds a bill line and an envelope drawdown, never a veto. (b) the MASK SPLIT remains open (not in this ruling).

F353 | api-places-enrichment | restaurant-location-enrichment.service.ts:2312-2448 (enrichSecondaryLocations) | THE `locationBias` PARAMETER OF SECONDARY EXPANSION IS DEAD, AND A RULED DESIGN IS BUILT ON THE BELIEF THAT IT IS LIVE. `enrichSecondaryLocations(entity, placeDetails, locationBias?)` has exactly ONE caller (:744) and it passes TWO arguments. `GooglePlacesService.findPlaceFromText` applies a bias only under `if (options.locationBias)`, so the live expansion is an UNBIASED GLOBAL text search for the brand name. plans/data-audit-2026-08.md's P2.2 research states the opposite as the cause of its class (b): "The brand is right; secondary expansion is LOCATION-BIASED so it never attached the other metro's branches" — and P2.2 rule 2 proposes spending a new per-mention Places call with the mention metro's bias to repair it. MEASURED on the local mirror, the premise does not hold: Chipotle 61 locations, Raising Cane's 58, Jet's Pizza 56, sweetgreen 39, Dunkin' 39 — all multi-metro, all one domain, all expanded. What the data DOES show is (i) 23 restaurants sit at or past the per-run cap of 60 with a maximum of 121, i.e. large brands hold an ARBITRARY, RANKING-DEPENDENT, TRUNCATED sample of their real branches (Chipotle has thousands), and (ii) a separate real class the research did not name: `Shake Shack 1700 Broadway - 53rd & 7th` has ONE location, because when Google's displayName for the grounding place is branch-qualified, the brand-purity gate `restaurantNamesAgree(canonicalName, siblingName)` rejects every genuine sibling. | LADDER: the dead parameter compensates for nothing — it is residue that makes the code READ as biased, which is how the research reached a wrong diagnosis; that is the real cost of dead code here. Bedrock, and the part that matters for the build: P2.2's metro test asks "does this brand have a location in the mention's metro?", and that question is only answerable against a COMPLETE location set. Today's set is knowingly truncated at 60-per-run and knowingly empty for branch-qualified brands, so the metro test would answer "no" for a real chain and MINT A TWIN — precisely the class-(b) failure P2.2's red team set out to avoid, reached by a different road. Rederive: before P2.2 ships, expansion must either be complete or must SAY it is incomplete — a per-brand `expansionComplete` fact (set false when a run hits the cap or the name gate rejected candidates), and the metro test must abstain (adopt, never mint) whenever it is false. The name gate should compare against the entity's BRAND name (normalizeBrandName already exists in this file) rather than Google's branch-qualified displayName. Red team: this does not weaken brand purity — an abstain is strictly safer than a mint — and it removes the need for rule 2's extra per-mention Places call in the common case, which is the cheaper answer to the same problem. | ESCALATED (corrects the premise of a RULED design before it is built) | PROOF: grep — one call site, two arguments; google-places.service.ts:637 `if (options.locationBias)`; psql on localhost/crave_search for the location counts, the 23-at-cap figure, and the Shake Shack row.

F354 | api-places-enrichment | restaurant-location-enrichment.service.ts:2442-2447 vs restaurant-secondary-location-expansion-queue.service.ts:49 | A RETRY POLICY THAT CAN NEVER FIRE, GUARDING A JOB THAT FAILS PARTWAY BY DESIGN. `enrichSecondaryLocations` wraps its entire paginated body — every Places call, every upsert — in one `catch` that logs a warning and returns void. The queue that drives it declares `attempts: 3`. Because the service can never throw, the job always completes successfully, so the three attempts are unreachable: a fault after page 1 leaves a brand permanently holding a partial branch set, the job is marked done, and nothing anywhere records that the set is partial. The failure and the success are the same observable. | LADDER: the catch compensates for the correct instinct that one brand's expansion must not kill the pass. Bedrock: not crashing and not telling anyone are different decisions, and only the first was made (the identical shape F205 names for the signals crons). Worse here, because the caller is a QUEUE that already implements the right behaviour — retry — and is prevented from using it. Rederive: let the loop throw; the queue's `attempts: 3` then means what it says, and per-page progress is already idempotent (upsert keyed on googlePlaceId, `seenPlaceIds` rebuilt from stored locations each run) so a retry resumes rather than repeats. If a swallow must remain, it must be visible in a RETURN value (`{complete: boolean}`) that the caller records — which is the same fact F353 needs for the metro test. Red team: throwing makes a vendor outage retry three times at Places cost instead of zero — that is the trade, and it is the one the queue's author already chose for the primary lane; the alternative (silent permanent truncation) is worse because it corrupts the location set that P2.2 will reason over. | FIXED (owner-ruled 2026-08-03, D32 escalation: YES to throwing) | PROOF: read; the catch at :2442 had no rethrow; queue `attempts: 3` at :49. || PASS-3 PROOF: the catch is GONE — `enrichSecondaryLocations` throws, the worker does not swallow, and the queue's attempts:3 is reachable for the first time. THE IDEMPOTENCE CLAIM WAS VERIFIED, NOT ASSUMED, and it was HALF FALSE: upserts keyed on the unique `googlePlaceId` and a `seenPlaceIds` rebuilt each attempt from a freshly re-read `include: { locations: true }` are real (single caller, restaurant-location-enrichment.service.ts:520-579), BUT the loop's `processedThisPage === 0` break meant a retry — whose page 1 is entirely already-stored — STOPPED AT PAGE 1 and never reached the page that faulted. The resume was a resume in name only. The break test now counts qualifying branches PRESENT on the page (`processedThisPage + alreadyStoredThisPage`), not rows WRITTEN, excluding the canonical place so a zero-branch brand still stops after page 1 and buys no second page. Two consequential side-effects, both named in code: `removeOnFail` 50->true (a retained failed job squats on the jobId and silently no-ops the next enqueue — harmless while nothing could fail, harmful the moment failure is real) and the primary lane's exponential backoff mirrored (three attempts back-to-back would triple a vendor blip's Places bill in seconds). Spend authority unchanged: same per-op ceilings, same 60-result cap. SPEC restaurant-secondary-location-expansion.spec.ts, 9 cases, 4 RED-proved by mutation: reinstating the worker's swallow, reverting the pagination-resume test, dropping the queue's campaign capture, and dropping the worker's runInWorkContext each fail exactly one case.

F355 | api-places-enrichment | restaurant-entity-merge.service.ts:183-219 (mergeLocations) + prisma/schema.prisma:136,154 | TWO SOURCES OF TRUTH FOR "THE PRIMARY LOCATION", AND THE UNCONSTRAINED ONE IS WRONG 398 TIMES. `core_entities.primary_location_id` is a real FK — structurally single. `core_restaurant_locations.is_primary` is a plain Boolean with only a non-unique index. MEASURED on the local mirror: 398 restaurants carry MORE THAN ONE `is_primary = true` row; and yet the FK and the boolean never disagree about WHICH row is primary (0 rows where the FK points at a non-primary, 0 restaurants with locations but no primary flag). So the boolean is not a different answer — it is the same answer plus 398 false extras. A plausible producer is right here: `mergeLocations` sets `isPrimary: location.isPrimary || canonicalLocations.length === 0` inside a loop over the duplicate's locations, against a snapshot taken BEFORE the loop — so when the canonical started with zero locations, EVERY moved row is flagged primary, and the "ensure a primary exists" step afterwards then picks one for the FK and leaves the rest flagged. Live consumers read the boolean: signals.service.ts:256 orders by `isPrimary desc` and takes the first, and four search projections serialize it. | LADDER: the boolean compensates for the FK not existing yet at the time it was written. Bedrock: "the primary location" is a single-valued property of the RESTAURANT, and it already has a single-valued home; a per-row Boolean is a representation that can express states the concept does not have, so it will eventually hold one. Rederive: delete the column and derive `isPrimary` at read as `location_id = entity.primary_location_id` (a one-line expression in the four projections). If the column must stay for query convenience, it needs a partial unique index `(restaurant_id) WHERE is_primary` so the 398 become impossible rather than merely unnoticed — and the 398 must be repaired first, which is what makes this a migration rather than a code change. Red team: deriving is strictly better (one truth, no drift, no migration risk beyond the drop); the index option is cheaper but keeps two facts in sync by constraint rather than by construction, and the merge bug would then throw instead of corrupting — loud, but it would need fixing anyway. | FIXED-PARTIAL (the PRODUCER is fixed 2026-08-03; the column drop + 398-row repair remain OWNER-DECISION) | PROOF(pass1): psql on localhost/crave_search — 398 restaurants with >1 is_primary; 0 FK/boolean disagreements across 6,848 restaurants with locations. || PASS-3 PROOF (D32): mergeLocations no longer decides primacy in the loop — moved rows land isPrimary:false, one primary is elected once, and every other row of the canonical is DEMOTED (updateMany), so the merge can no longer ADD primaries. The 398 existing rows and the column drop need a migration plus edits across the SEARCH module (~40 sites), which is outside this pass's four modules — STOPPED and reported rather than half-migrated.

F356 | api-places-enrichment | restaurant-enrichment-queue.service.ts + restaurant-cuisine-extraction-queue.service.ts + restaurant-secondary-location-expansion-queue.service.ts | THREE QUEUE SERVICES ARE ONE CONCEPT WRITTEN THREE TIMES, AND EVERY DIVERGENCE BETWEEN THEM IS AN ACCIDENT. The cuisine and expansion queues are byte-equivalent apart from a two-part job id — same `buildJobId`, same `isDuplicateJobError`, same options. Against the primary-enrichment queue they differ on four axes, none of them deliberate: (1) `removeOnFail: true` vs `50` — the primary queue DOCUMENTS why true is right ("Failed jobs must not squat on the jobId and silently no-op later enqueues"), and its two siblings keep 50 failed jobs that do exactly that squatting; (2) `backoff: exponential` on the primary lane only; (3) `currentCampaignId()` captured on the primary lane only (F352); (4) an `isDuplicateJobError` helper — duplicated verbatim in both siblings — that matches on the vendor prose `'already exists'` and, since Bull's `add` with an existing jobId RESOLVES with the existing job rather than throwing, appears to be a guard for a case that does not occur. | LADDER: compensates for there being no shared enqueue primitive, so each lane was written by copying the previous one and editing what the author happened to think about. Bedrock: "enqueue idempotent work for entity X on queue Q" is one operation with one policy; the only things that legitimately vary are the queue name and the payload. Rederive: one `IdempotentEntityQueue` base (or a factory) owning jobId construction, removeOnComplete/removeOnFail, attempts, backoff and work-context capture; the three services become three declarations. The `isDuplicateJobError` string match either proves it can fire (with a test) or is deleted. Red team: this deletes ~90 lines and, more importantly, makes the next queue inherit the four lessons instead of re-learning them; the risk is that one of the divergences was intentional and undocumented, which is exactly why the rederivation must make each policy an explicit named choice rather than a default nobody stated. | FIXED-PARTIAL (dead guard deleted 2026-08-03; the base-class unification STOPPED per stop-if-wrong) | PROOF(pass1): read all three side by side; grep for `currentCampaignId` and `removeOnFail`. || PASS-3 PROOF (D32): the `isDuplicateJobError` string match is DEAD and is deleted from both siblings, with the vendor fact recorded — bull 4.16's addJob-6.lua checks `EXISTS jobIdKey` and RETURNS the existing id, it never throws 'already exists' (read at node_modules/bull/lib/commands/addJob-6.lua:57-59). That removed ~36 lines of unreachable code and both services' now-unused logger. STOPPED on the base class: after the deletion the two siblings are ~57 lines each, so a base + three thin subclasses would ADD lines, not delete them — and the two divergences the base was meant to unify (removeOnFail 50-vs-true, campaignId capture) both change WHEN Places money is spent and are the escalated F354 question. Each divergence is now a NAMED choice with its reason in place.

F357 | api-places-enrichment | attribute-ontology.service.ts:160-290 + attribute-ontology.worker.ts:43 | THE ADJUDICATION LOOP HAS NO CEILING ON ITS OWN SIZE, AND ITS COST IS LINEAR IN A NUMBER NOBODY CHOSE. `buildPlan` issues one `placeAttribute` LLM call PER CANDIDATE (pass 1, concurrency 12), one more per NEW canonical (pass 2), and one `chooseAttributeName` per new canonical that absorbed synonyms (pass 3) — plus one embedding pass over every candidate AND every active canonical. There is no batch cap, no spend campaign, and no early exit; `candidateCount` is simply however many `pending` rows collection happened to mint. The worker fires this on a 60s debounce after every collection batch, and `scope: 'all'` re-embeds and re-places the ENTIRE vocabulary. The individual calls ARE governed (llm.service routes both through `callerProfile('attribute.place')` / `('attribute.canonicalize_name')` and the gated client, so the monthly spend gate can stop them), so this is not an ungoverned-spend hole like F352 — it is an UNBOUNDED WORK hole: a re-extraction that mints tens of thousands of pending attributes turns one debounce tick into a run whose size and duration nobody declared, competing for the same Gemini pool as collection itself. | LADDER: compensates for the quarantine design being genuinely correct — because pending attributes are invisible until adjudicated, correctness never depends on this finishing, so no ceiling ever felt necessary. Bedrock: correctness-independence justifies being LATE, not being UNBOUNDED; a job whose cost scales with a queue nobody bounds needs to state its own per-run size, and the ledger already prices these calls. Rederive: a per-run candidate cap (drain oldest-pending-first, leave the rest for the next tick — the debounce already re-fires), so the run size is a declared number rather than an emergent one; and, since the reload/re-extract campaigns already carry spend envelopes, adjudication triggered during a campaign should ride that campaign like the enrichment lane does. Red team: a cap adds latency for new vocabulary going live, which the module's own doc calls "purely the latency knob" — i.e. exactly the property it is willing to trade. The alternative is that the first big re-extraction discovers the run size empirically, on the bill. | FIXED (bounded by an EXISTING pool 2026-08-03; the per-run SIZE cap refused as a new constant) | PROOF(pass1): read; no cap between :169 fetchAttributeRows (unfiltered by count) and the batch loop at :217; llm.service.ts:1369/:1954 confirm both calls carry caller profiles. || PASS-3 PROOF (D32): the adjudication job payload carries the enqueuing work's campaignId and the worker re-establishes it via runInWorkContext, so a run triggered during a reload/re-extract campaign debits that campaign's envelope (usage-ledger's meterCampaignSpend already attributes ambiently) and breaches like any other lane. NO new constant. The per-run candidate cap D32 also floated is NOT implemented and cannot be: every per-run ceiling is a number nobody has measured, and no existing governed pool expresses a run SIZE — recorded in buildPlan's docstring rather than invented.

F358 | api-places-enrichment | estimators/estimator-registry.ts:204-228, 63-65, 23-30, 94 | THE EXPLORATION MECHANISM THE REGISTRY _FORCES_ YOU TO DECLARE IS INVERTED AT THE ONE CASE IT EXISTS FOR — PLUS TWO DECLARED LAWS WITH NO IMPLEMENTATION AND ONE DEAD FIELD. `register()` refuses any self-gating estimator that does not declare an exploration mechanism (a genuinely good structural law). But `read()` computes `uncertainty = sqrt(variance / total)` from OBSERVED variance alone, so a subject with ZERO observations gets variance 0 and therefore uncertainty 0 — maximum confidence from no data. `EstimatorReading.uncertainty`'s own docstring says "Infinity when no data and no prior", and the staged-off branch twenty lines above correctly returns Infinity; the enabled branch returns 0. For `optimisticSelection` — documented as "a starved candidate can always re-demonstrate" — the upper-confidence bound of a never-observed subject is exactly the prior, which is BELOW that of any subject with real variance, so the starved candidate is ranked LAST. The mechanism does the opposite of its stated job. Separately: `versionBindings` is declared by 4 configs and read by NOTHING, and no API exists to change a bound version — so the header's law "a bound version change widens uncertainty / flags re-probe instead of silently mixing regimes" is asserted in prose and implemented nowhere; `hierarchy` is declared with four meanings ("Place-keyed estimators shrink along the places containment DAG") and never read by the registry — the shrinkage lives in PollSupplyEstimators.hierarchicalRead, which takes name+placeId explicitly and ignores `config.hierarchy`, so a second consumer calling plain `read()` on a 'placesDag' estimator silently gets no shrinkage and nothing says so; and `weightSquares` is incremented and decayed on every observation and never read at all. | LADDER: all four compensate for a primitive that was specified more completely than it was built (one commit, "Phase A"), and the type then made the unbuilt parts look built. Bedrock: a config field is a PROMISE the reader will honour; a field the reader ignores is worse than a missing feature because it silently converts a caller's correct declaration into a no-op. Rederive: (1) uncertainty must account for the prior's own dispersion so that n=0 yields the WIDEST interval, not the narrowest — the minimal honest form is to floor it by a prior-derived term that decays as nEffective grows, which is the same self-erasure the estimate already implements; (2) either implement version binding (a `bindVersion(name, value)` that widens or resets on change) or DELETE `versionBindings` and the header clause — a law with no enforcement is the always-green disease at the level of documentation; (3) move `hierarchicalRead` INTO the registry keyed off `config.hierarchy`, so declaring 'placesDag' does the thing (the poll module's implementation is the working reference); (4) delete `weightSquares`. Red team: (1) changes the numbers every consumer reads, so it needs the poll-supply specs re-baselined and is the only part with real blast radius; (2)-(4) are pure deletions or a lift-and-shift with none. | FIXED (D31, 2026-08-03) for limb (1); limbs (2)-(4) are OUT OF D31's SCOPE and still open | PROOF(pass1): EXECUTED (scratch spec, removed) — a never-observed subject on an `optimisticSelection` config returns `uncertainty = 0, priorWeight = 1`, and its UCB is 0.2 against a measured subject's 0.22, i.e. the starved candidate loses. Both assertions PASSED. grep confirms zero readers for `versionBindings`, `weightSquares`, and `config.hierarchy`. || PASS-3 PROOF (D31): uncertainty is Infinity whenever dispersion is UNMEASURED — a staged-off reader, or fewer than two effective observations (a spread needs two points; the threshold is definitional, not tuned). A zero-observation subject now carries the widest interval and WINS an optimistic-selection comparison. RED-proved: 3 of the 4 new estimator-registry cases fail against the pre-D31 registry (git stash + rerun). Consumers already guarded for non-finite uncertainty (poll-supply-controller.ts:139, poll-weekly-ritual.service.ts:920); poll-supply + weekly-ritual suites green unchanged. STILL OPEN: versionBindings, config.hierarchy and weightSquares remain declared-and-unread — D31 ruled only on the exploration inversion.

F362 | api-places-enrichment | places/ip-location.service.ts:27-35, 49-50 | THE ONE FILE IN THIS TERRITORY THAT PREDATES THE REDERIVATION, AND IT SHOWS. `isPrivateOrLoopbackIp` is a hand-rolled prefix list (`10.`, `192.168.`, the 172.16-31 regex, `fc`/`fd`, two IPv4-mapped forms) that misses 169.254.0.0/16 link-local, 100.64.0.0/10 CGNAT, `::ffff:10.` / `::ffff:192.168.` (it special-cases ONLY the mapped loopback), and IPv6 unique-local written in upper case. The vendor base URL and the 2.5s timeout are class literals rather than config, unlike every other vendor surface in this module, and the provider is called with no governance pool, no usage-ledger row and no cache — the launch-position controller's own comment concedes this ("an anonymous, untiered way to burn a third-party quota (and, past the free tier, money)") and treats a rate-limit tier as the whole answer. | LADDER: the prefix list compensates for not wanting a dependency to answer "is this address routable". Rederive: use the `ipaddr.js`-style range check (or `net.isIP` plus the IANA table declared once), and put this vendor on the same footing as every other one in the module — a governed pool draw and a usage-ledger row, so it appears in cost-reconcile at all. The vendor is STILL uncached, ungoverned and off the usage ledger (F362's second half): adding a pool changes when a vendor call is refused, which was outside this pass's no-spend-behaviour mandate.

F363 | api-places-enrichment | google-place-type-attributes.ts:96+ (RESTAURANT_ATTRIBUTE_VOCAB) vs restaurant-location-enrichment.service.ts:126+ (GOOGLE_RESTAURANT_ATTRIBUTE_DEFINITIONS) | A FILE THAT DECLARES ITSELF THE SINGLE SOURCE OF TRUTH IS ONE OF TWO SOURCES, AND THEY HAVE ALREADY DRIFTED. google-place-type-attributes.ts states: "SINGLE SOURCE OF TRUTH: entities are created on demand with these aliases by RestaurantLocationEnrichmentService.ensureRestaurantAttributeEntity — no seed step, no maintenance." But the Google boolean-field attributes are declared a SECOND time, with their own alias arrays, inside the enrichment service. MEASURED: 4 of the 8 boolean attributes have divergent alias lists, and the drift is systematic — the service copy is missing the bare single-word alias in every case (`dogs`, `pets` for "allows dogs"; `children` for "good for children"; `groups` for "good for groups"; `sports` for "good for watching sports"). Which alias set an attribute entity ends up with therefore depends on which code path minted it. This is the repo's own "type-list disease" (F110) on a vocabulary that feeds resolution and autocomplete. | LADDER: compensates for the two declarations serving what LOOK like different purposes — one is name+aliases, the other adds an `isEnabled(place)` predicate — so the second was written rather than the first extended. Bedrock: they are the same vocabulary; the predicate is one more field of an entry, not a reason for a second table. Rederive: `RESTAURANT_ATTRIBUTE_VOCAB` entries gain an optional `isEnabled?: (place) => boolean`, the enrichment service imports and filters on it, and `GOOGLE_RESTAURANT_ATTRIBUTE_DEFINITIONS` is deleted — the file's existing derivation style (ALIASES_BY_NAME and CANONICAL_NAMES are already computed from their sources, which is why THOSE have not drifted) is exactly right and just needs to cover this last table. Red team: the merge must take the UNION of the two alias lists so the five bare aliases are not lost, and any attribute entity already minted from the narrower list keeps its stored aliases — so a one-off alias backfill is the honest completion, or the drift simply persists in data while the code stops producing more of it. | FIXED (2026-08-03) | PROOF(pass1): EXECUTED — a python diff of the two alias arrays per canonicalName reports agree for delivery/takeout/dine in/curbside pickup and DRIFT for allows dogs / good for children / good for groups / good for watching sports, with the missing member printed in each case. || PASS-3 PROOF: GOOGLE_RESTAURANT_ATTRIBUTE_DEFINITIONS is DELETED; RestaurantAttributeVocabEntry gained an optional isEnabled(place) and GOOGLE_BOOLEAN_ATTRIBUTE_VOCAB is DERIVED from the one table (the same derivation style that kept ALIASES_BY_NAME from drifting). The union restored the 8 bare aliases the service copy had lost (dogs, pets, children, groups, sports, outdoor, outside, vegetarian restaurant). SURPRISE: the union could NOT be taken blindly — the service copy aliased 'cafe'/'café' to 'serves coffee', but 'cafe' is a CANONICAL attribute of its own in the vocabulary (Google's `cafe` place type). An alias may not outrank a canonical name, so 'café' joined the canonical it spells and 'cafe' was refused; a new no-collision spec is what caught it.

LIMIT 200 is now PREFIX_LANE_PER_RUN_CEILING with its evidence: MEASURED on the local mirror 2026-08-03, the prefix lane's full predicate admits SEVEN pairs corpus-wide (BEGIN/ROLLBACK probe), so 200 is not a bound on normal operation but a one-night churn ceiling if the lane ever widens by an order of magnitude — and the exact-identity lane stays uncapped because an exact key match cannot widen.

F365 | api-places-enrichment | restaurant-janitor.service.ts:57, 62-63, 89-90 | THE LIFECYCLE PASS READS process.env DIRECTLY, THREE TIMES, WITH NO VALIDATION — IN A REPO THAT HAS TWICE RULED THAT A SETTING HAS ONE READER. `LOCATION_LIFECYCLE_CRON_ENABLED`, `LOCATION_REFRESH_TTL_DAYS` and `LOCATION_REFRESH_LIMIT` are read off `process.env` inside the cron body rather than through ConfigService, and the two numeric ones are coerced with bare `Number(...)`, so a typo'd value yields NaN and is handed straight to `refreshStaleLocations`, where `olderThanDays ?? 90` does NOT catch it (NaN is not nullish) — `Date.now() - NaN*86400000` is an Invalid Date and the `lastPolledAt < cutoff` filter then matches nothing, silently disabling the DETECT half of the lifecycle forever with no error. The janitor reads ConfigService; `process.env` no longer appears in the file. The two knobs that decide who is ARCHIVED (noMatchAttemptThreshold, retryLimit) are configurable now too — F365's symmetry complaint. Defaults unchanged (90/250/3/25), so a correct box behaves identically.

[]` are gone — a single vectorFor(name) THROWS if the map misses, saying the contract out loud instead of degrading semantic recall to 'the first k canonicals in arbitrary order'; buildShortlist's empty-vector branch deleted with it. Both invented thresholds are MEASURED against the live vocabulary (411 active restaurant_attribute entities, 84,255 pairs, local mirror 2026-08-03): the 0.4 trigram floor admits 56 pairs, ALL real near-duplicates, where 0.3 admits 205 (noise) and 0.5 loses cocktails/serves cocktails and breakfast/serves breakfast — the exact collisions adjudication exists for; the 3-character token floor is what stops the 18 pairs joined only by a function word ('no frills'/'no cash', 'wine on tap'/'on a boat'), and 4 would drop 305 real pairs. 19-case spec built from those measured pairs.

F369 | api-places-enrichment | restaurant-cuisine-extraction.service.ts:18, 121, 155 vs :80-110 | A FACT IS COMPUTED, STORED, AND NEVER READ — AND IT IS EXACTLY THE FACT THAT WOULD ANSWER THE QUESTION THE CODE ASKS. Cuisine extraction is once-ever per restaurant: the early return is gated on `extractedAt` alone, so a restaurant whose Google editorial summary later changes is never re-extracted. The service nonetheless hashes that summary into `summaryHash` and persists it in the extraction metadata. Repo-wide grep finds exactly three occurrences — the type declaration, the computation, and the write. Nothing ever compares it. So the machinery for "has the evidence changed?" was built and wired to nothing, and the gate that should consume it uses a timestamp instead. | LADDER: compensates for re-extraction policy not having been decided — the hash was written in anticipation of a rule nobody wrote. Bedrock: a stored derived value with no reader is not a feature in waiting, it is a claim about a future nobody committed to (F112's framing, verbatim). Two legal shapes and the owner picks: either the gate becomes `extractedAt && summaryHash === currentHash` so a changed summary re-extracts (which costs one LLM call per changed restaurant per refresh, on a corpus where refreshStaleLocations already re-polls Google every 90 days), or the hash is deleted and once-ever is stated as the deliberate policy. Red team: the first makes the once-ever rule honest and self-healing but couples cuisine spend to Google's editorial churn, which is unmeasured — so measure it first (how many stored summaries would differ today?) before choosing; the second is free and loses nothing that exists. | FIXED by DELETION (2026-08-03) | PROOF(pass1): `grep -rn summaryHash apps/api/src` returns 3 lines, all in this file, none of them a read. || PASS-3 PROOF: banking hunt first — repo-wide grep over apps/, packages/ and scripts/ found the same three occurrences and no reader anywhere, mobile included. summaryHash, its computation and the now-unused hashValue/createHash are DELETED, and once-ever is stated as the deliberate policy at the gate with the reason: option A costs one LLM call per changed restaurant per refresh cycle and Google's editorial-summary churn is UNMEASURED — measuring it needs a Places re-poll, i.e. spend. Rows written before today keep the key in their free-form blob; nothing reads it there either.

F370 | api-places-enrichment | restaurant-janitor-policy.spec.ts (all 4 cases) | MUTATION-PROVED ALWAYS-GREEN: THE ONLY SPEC GUARDING WHO GETS ARCHIVED CANNOT SEE THE POLICY. The file is a REGEX SCAN OF SOURCE TEXT — it pins the column name (`enrichment_failure_count`) and the increment shape, and asserts nothing about the comparison operator, the threshold, the `status IN ('no_match','error')` filter, the `google_place_id IS NULL` predicate (which is the guard the "$118 lesson" bought), or the retry arm. EXECUTED MUTATION: flip `AND enrichment_failure_count >= ${threshold}` to `< ${threshold}` in restaurant-janitor.service.ts — the janitor now archives every HEALTHY placeholder and archives nothing that actually failed, i.e. it destroys exactly the entities it exists to preserve. Result: 4 passed, 4 total, STILL GREEN. The spec cannot distinguish "reads the right field" from "reads the right field and does the opposite with it". This is the disease CLAUDE.md's ratified methodology names, applied to entity destruction. | LADDER: compensates for the policy living in a raw SQL string, which a unit test cannot execute without a database — so the author tested the only thing reachable, the text. Bedrock: a policy that decides whether a row is archived is BEHAVIOUR, and behaviour is proven by running it; a scan of the source is a spelling check. The file's own subject matter is the reason — its header records that the counter was previously a JSON path whose writer meant something else entirely, a bug a text scan would also have passed. Rederive: this belongs in the integration lane beside places-containment.integration.spec.ts — seed a handful of restaurants across the four policy states (below threshold, at threshold, grounded, no-match vs error) against the real Postgres and assert WHICH ids come back. The repo already has the harness, the database and the CI step; it has one file in it. Red team: an integration test is slower and needs DATABASE_URL, and F351 shows that lane is currently unwatched — so this finding and F351 must land together or neither is worth much. | FIXED (D30, 2026-08-03) | PROOF(pass1): EXECUTED mutation + `npx jest --testPathPattern='restaurant-janitor-policy'` → 4 passed on the inverted comparison; production file reverted via `git checkout --`, `git diff --stat` empty. || PASS-3 PROOF (D30): restaurant-janitor-policy.integration.spec.ts seeds nine restaurants across every policy state and asserts WHICH IDS each arm selected — JanitorSummary gained `selected` for exactly this. 8 cases, green against the real Postgres via `yarn test:db` (which CI already runs). RED-PROVED with the finding's own mutation: `>= threshold` → `<= threshold` fails 2 cases; deleting the `google_place_id IS NULL` NOT EXISTS fails 2 including the $118-law case. The old file keeps ONLY its writer half, relabelled a lint, with the always-green history in its header.

F371 | api-places-enrichment | places-catalog.service.spec.ts:534 ("the viewport read is BOUNDED") | MUTATION-PROVED ALWAYS-GREEN: THE ABUSE FIX'S OWN TEST CANNOT SEE THE BOUND. `PLACES_IN_VIEW_CANDIDATE_CAP = 400` exists because an unbounded world-span read seq-scanned every ground and serialized 11 MB of GeoJSON per request from an endpoint reachable at 100/min. The test that guards it asserts `expect(sql).toContain('LIMIT')`. EXECUTED MUTATION: set the cap to 1 — the §2.5 dominator law now sees a single candidate and the header verdict silently changes at every zoom. Result: places-catalog + places.controller = 33 passed, STILL GREEN. Any cap value satisfies the assertion, including one that breaks the law the cap was chosen to protect. Related, same file: the crossing-view tests count arms (`match(/geometry && /g).length === 2` + `toContain(' OR ')`) — they would catch the ST_Union regression they were written for, but not two arms with identical or wrong bounds; and :522/:555/:711 assert literal source strings (`'/*places:grounds_in_view*/'`, `'ST_Covers'`, `'ORDER BY ST_Area(pg.geometry) ASC'`), which are tripwires against edits, not tests of meaning. | LADDER: compensates for the semantics living in SQL — and the author KNEW it, which is why places-containment.integration.spec.ts exists and says so in its header ("a real bug shipped on 2026-07-27: `geometry && ST_Union(armA, armB)` looked right and its spec asserted `toContain('ST_Union')`, so the spec PASSED ON THE BUG"). The lesson was learned, the integration file was written — and then the unit specs kept the same string-matching shape for everything the integration file does not cover, and the integration file stopped running (F351). Bedrock: a constant that encodes a LAW ("at most 3 places can hold attention, so the cap can never drop a candidate that could have won") should be tested against the law, not against its own presence. Rederive: assert the RELATIONSHIP the doc claims — with the cap in force and N grounds ordered by area, the set returned still contains every place that could clear 1/3 of the view. That is provable against the real PostGIS in the integration lane, and is the same assertion that would catch a cap of 1. Red team: same dependency as F370 — the honest home for these is the lane F351 just made runnable again. | FIXED (D30, 2026-08-03) | PROOF(pass1): EXECUTED mutation `PLACES_IN_VIEW_CANDIDATE_CAP` 400 → 1 → 33 passed; reverted, `git diff --stat` empty. || PASS-3 PROOF (D30): places-viewport-cap.integration.spec.ts seeds PLACES_IN_VIEW_CANDIDATE_CAP + 55 grounds in an empty Pacific view — five that clear the 1/3 attention floor and 450 tail places — and asserts the LAW the cap is justified by: every place that could have won still survives the cap. RED-PROVED: setting the cap to 1 fails it (the mutation 33 unit tests passed on). The unit case at places-catalog.service.spec.ts is retitled 'SHAPE ONLY' and points at the integration proof.

F372 | api-places-enrichment | tomtom-chain-probe.adapter.spec.ts ("qualifies the forward-geocode query by name + subdivision") + place-dag-read.spec.ts + places-reconciler.service.spec.ts | THREE SMALLER TEST HOLES, RECORDED SO THEY ARE NOT RE-DISCOVERED. (a) The adapter spec's stated distinction is not tested: its comment says the Municipality rung "carries the county qualifier… while the state rung stays unqualified", but BOTH assertions are the identical string `endsWith('/New York, NY.json')`, and the fixture's `countrySecondarySubdivision: 'New York'` is set up and never discriminated. (b) place-dag-read.ts documents two performance/correctness facts as load-bearing — `UNION` not `UNION ALL` ("a defective cycle terminates instead of looping") and `@>` not `= ANY` (the measured 13-17s → 28ms GIN attribution, which was the NY home-feed latency) — and the spec greps only `'WITH RECURSIVE subtree'`, so reverting either passes. Its first case, `expect(result).toEqual([STATE, TOWN])`, is pure mock pass-through; only the dedupe assertion carries information. (c) The reconciler's 30-day TTL has no expiry test: the spec proves a cached region answers WITHIN the TTL, and the prune test only counts `deleteMany` calls — nothing proves an EXPIRED region re-probes, which is the arm that costs money when it is wrong in either direction (never re-probing suppresses discovery for 30 days; always re-probing is the spend-forever class the memory exists to prevent). Also noted: `places-promotion.service.spec.ts`'s "a wrong-entity rejection meters its draws" survives removal of the wrong-entity guard entirely, because it asserts only `recordSpend`, which fires on both branches — it is a sound metering test wearing a misleading name. | LADDER: (a) and (b) compensate for nothing — they are assertions written to the shape of the code rather than the shape of the claim. (c) compensates for time being hard to test, which `jest.useFakeTimers` or an injected clock answers. Bedrock: for every fact a docblock states as load-bearing, some test must fail when it stops being true — the docblocks here are unusually good, which is exactly what makes the untested ones dangerous, because a reader trusts them. Rederive: (a) assert the two query strings DIFFER; (b) assert the built SQL contains `@>` and not `= ANY`, and add a cyclic-DAG fixture that terminates; (c) advance the clock past NEGATIVE_OBSERVATION_TTL_MS and assert a probe fires. All three are cheap and stay in the unit lane. | ESCALATED (test-only) | PROOF: subagent review with executed runs; the promotion-spec claim verified by the M3 mutation (anchor-containment guard removed → that test still passed while a different one went red).

F129 | mobile-app-core | apps/mobile/src/screens/Onboarding.tsx:91-128, 688-693, 1697-1747, 1749-1800 | FOUND WHILE IMPLEMENTING D7; **NOT TOUCHED — needs its own owner ruling.** The onboarding CAROUSEL (the screen that HOSTS the teaser step, not the teaser step itself) renders a personalised economics story built entirely from invented constants: `FREQUENCY_TO_MONTHLY` (rarely 6 / weekly 14 / often 22 / daily 28), `BUDGET_TO_AMOUNT` (15/30/55/80), `FREQUENCY_RANGES`, `baselineRegretRate = 0.35`, `craveRegretRate = 0.08`, a bare `* 0.33`, plus rendered claims "11 in 12 meals hit", "4x less time and effort", "Missing intel (35%)", "$N/mo wasted" and "you'll redirect ~$N/month". Every one of these is a numeric claim about the user's own life, presented as a derived figure, with no measurement behind any of it — the no-fake-estimates law's central violation, at larger scale than anything inside the teaser. WHY IT WAS LEFT ALONE: the owner's ruling names "the teaser screen", and the Phase-3 brief scoped D7 to `apps/api/src/modules/teaser` + the teaser's mobile consumers. Deleting these would remove whole designed onboarding STEPS and their copy — a product change, not a rederivation. | LADDER: compensates for having no measured retention/regret data at pitch-writing time. Bedrock: a number shown to a user is a fact, an owner choice, or a derivation from one of those; a persuasion figure is none of the three. Rederive: either the owner ratifies each constant as a stated marketing claim (and it stops being presented as _their_ number), or the steps make their case in words and drop the arithmetic. | OWNER-DECISION (escalated by the implementation pass) | read; grep of the constants and their four render sites

F130 | api-identity-money | apps/api/src/modules/teaser/teaser.service.ts:136-143 | RE-ESCALATED, deliberately: `MIN_MENTIONS = 3` and `MIN_RESULTS = 3` survived the D7 batch. But MIN_MENTIONS's entire stated justification ("so a 2-opinion 9.9 can never headline the first thing a user ever sees") is a claim about the SCORE the teaser does show, and neither figure has a measurement or a recorded owner choice behind it. Deleting them would change which rows a real user sees — a product decision the ruling did not make. | Bedrock unchanged from F109: an evidence floor is a product decision or a derivation from the real (unbuilt) global floor. | OWNER-DECISION | read
