F400 | api-core | apps/api/src/shared/logging/logger.service.ts (whole file) vs logger.interface.ts + winston-logger.service.ts + shared.module.ts:22-25 | **THE API SENDS NO LOGGED ERROR TO SENTRY, AND THE SPEC THAT WOULD CATCH THAT TESTS AN ORPHAN.** There are TWO classes named `LoggerService`. (a) `logging/logger.interface.ts` — an `abstract class`, which is what `shared.module.ts` binds (`{provide: LoggerService, useExisting: WinstonLoggerService}`), what `shared/index.ts` re-exports, and what every service injects. (b) `logging/logger.service.ts` — a `@Injectable()` CONCRETE class carrying `captureToSentry`, whose header states "everything the codebase considers an error-level event ... flows through this one method, so Sentry capture lives here and nowhere else." Nothing imports (b) except its own spec. The DI-wired logger is `WinstonLoggerService`, which extends (a) and has NO Sentry code at all. So every HTTP 500 (GlobalExceptionFilter → `logger.error`), every cron/background catch, and every boot failure is written to stdout and goes NOWHERE else. Only `main.ts`'s process-level `unhandledRejection`/`uncaughtException` handlers and `debug.controller.ts` reach Sentry. Three separate comments assert the working seam and all three are false: `sentry.module.ts:9` ("The one capture seam is LoggerService.error (shared/logging/logger.service.ts)"), `global-exception.filter.ts:196` ("error level IS the Sentry seam — LoggerService.error captures"), and the seam spec's own preamble ("a refactor that severs the seam turns the suite red"). `git log` shows commit 7a2d83877 "crash reporting — one-seam design, both apps" added `captureToSentry` to the orphan — the API seam has never worked. This is the always-green-metric disease CLAUDE.md names, in the crash reporter itself. | LADDER: the duplicate class compensates for nothing — it is a pre-existing concrete logger that was superseded by the abstract-interface + Winston pair and never deleted, leaving a plausible-looking file for a later change to land in. Bedrock: a seam is not a seam because a comment says so; it is a seam because the SHIPPED object goes through it. A spec that constructs its subject with `new` instead of resolving it from the container is testing a class, not a seam. Rederive: (1) delete `logging/logger.service.ts` entirely — its `LogMetadata` is a verbatim duplicate of the interface's (F415) and its sanitizers are duplicated in `winston-logger.service.ts`; (2) move `captureToSentry` into `WinstonLoggerService.error`, the one shipped implementation; (3) rewrite `logger-sentry-seam.spec.ts` to obtain its logger from a compiled `Test.createTestingModule({imports:[SharedModule]}).get(LoggerService)` so the assertion binds to the WIRING, not to a constructor — that is the only version that can go red on this defect; (4) sequence it AFTER F407, or the repair turns every 404 into a Sentry event. Red team: the new spec fails if anyone re-binds `LoggerService` to something without capture, which is the actual invariant; the residual risk is Sentry volume, which F407 owns. | FIXED (D33) — ONE LoggerService. `captureToSentry` moved into `WinstonLoggerService.error`, the class `shared.module.ts` actually binds to the `LoggerService` token; the orphan `logging/logger.service.ts` (365 lines, incl. the duplicate `LogMetadata` and duplicate sanitizers of F415) is DELETED; the three false comments corrected (sentry.module.ts now names winston-logger.service.ts). Landed AFTER F407 so the repair does not meter 4xx. | **PROOF (executed, mutation-proven BOTH ways):** `logger-sentry-seam.spec.ts` rewritten to resolve its subject from a COMPILED `Test.createTestingModule({imports:[ConfigModule.forRoot(),SharedModule]}).get(LoggerService)` — it binds to the WIRING, not to a constructor. 7 pass, incl. a case replaying the GlobalExceptionFilter's own 5xx call shape. RED PROOF: deleting the single `this.captureToSentry(...)` line from WinstonLoggerService.error turns the suite RED — 4 failed / 3 passed, 'Expected number of calls: 1, Received number of calls: 0'. Restored → 10/10 green (with the F407 spec). Boot verified green on :3399 (health=200, Nest started, no errors), the shared dev API on :3000 untouched.

F401 | api-core | apps/api/src/shared/utils/process-role.ts:44-49 | **THE GLOBAL SPEND KILL-SWITCH USES THE EXACT DIALECT `env-flag.ts` WAS WRITTEN TO EXTERMINATE.** `isSchedulerRuntime()` tests `(process.env.CRONS_ENABLED ?? '').trim().toLowerCase() === 'false'`, so only the literal `false`/`FALSE` disables crons. Its own doc-comment calls it the switch for "environments that must never spend unattended (staging holds dev vendor keys)". `env-flag.ts` exists ~40 lines away and lists `=== 'false'` for an inverted-sense flag as one of the four dialects it replaces; MEMORY.md flags `CRONS_ENABLED=false` as a prod flag to watch. An operator typing the off-spelling this codebase's own helper accepts gets the crons RUNNING. | LADDER: compensates for the flag predating `isEnvFlagEnabled`. Bedrock: one spelling of "is this env var on", and a switch whose failure mode is unattended vendor spend must fail toward OFF. Rederive: `if (!isEnvFlagEnabled(process.env.CRONS_ENABLED, true)) return false;` — a single call, semantics preserved (absent ⇒ on), every off-spelling honoured, and an unrecognised value is OFF rather than a silent yes. Add the case to `env-parsing.spec.ts` beside the `COLLECTION_SCHEDULER_ENABLED` case it is a repeat of. Red team: the only behaviour change is that more spellings turn crons OFF — strictly the safe direction for a spend switch. | FIXED (D33) — `isSchedulerRuntime()` reads `isEnvFlagEnabled(process.env.CRONS_ENABLED, true)`: absent still means ON in a worker runtime, every off-spelling the canonical reader accepts now disables, and an unrecognized value is OFF (a kill-switch fails toward OFF). OPS NOTE: prod's current `CRONS_ENABLED=false` spelling is UNAFFECTED — it meant off before and means off now. | **PROOF (executed, mutation-proven):** `env-parsing.spec.ts` gained a `CRONS_ENABLED` describe beside the COLLECTION_SCHEDULER_ENABLED case it repeats — each of `false/FALSE/False/' false '/0/no/off/''` asserted to actually disable, `maybe` OFF, absent and `true` ON. RED PROOF: restoring the hand-rolled `=== 'false'` turns the dialect cases RED (part of a 15-failure mutation run across F401+F402). Restored → 37/37 green.

F402 | api-core | apps/api/src/shared/config/debug-routes.gate.ts:20-24 | **THE FAIL-CLOSED GATE READS `APP_ENV` RAW, SO FOUR SPELLINGS OF PRODUCTION DEFEAT IT.** The gate compares `env.APP_ENV === 'prod' || env.APP_ENV === 'staging'` and `env.NODE_ENV === 'production'` as literals, while `normalizeAppEnv` (imported by everything else) accepts `production`, `PROD`, `stage`, `STAGING`. `env-parsing.spec.ts` documents that these very spellings once forked Redis key namespaces. Its spec file asserts only the exact literals, so the gap is invisible. Separately, the opt-in is `ENABLE_DEBUG_ROUTES === 'true'`, a fifth dialect — `=1` is a silent no-op (the spec pins this deliberately, but it is a different rule from every other flag in the codebase). | LADDER: compensates for the gate wanting to be import-free and directly unit-testable — a good instinct that produced a hand-rolled env read instead of the shared one. Bedrock: "am I production" has exactly one answer in this codebase, and it is `isProdEnv(resolveAppEnv(env))`; importing a 60-line pure function does not drag in the dependency graph the file's header warns about. Rederive: `if (isDeployedEnv(resolveAppEnv(env))) return false; return isEnvFlagEnabled(env.ENABLE_DEBUG_ROUTES);` — three imports, all pure, all already unit-tested, and the NODE_ENV clause becomes redundant because `resolveAppEnv` already infers prod from it. Extend the spec with the spellings (`production`, `PROD`, `stage`) that currently pass. Red team: strictly more environments are refused; the one behaviour change is `ENABLE_DEBUG_ROUTES=1` now working locally, which is the point of a shared flag reader. | FIXED (D33) — the gate is now `if (isDeployedEnv(resolveAppEnv(env))) return false; return isEnvFlagEnabled(env.ENABLE_DEBUG_ROUTES);` — three pure, already-unit-tested imports. The separate NODE_ENV clause is gone because `resolveAppEnv` already infers prod from it, and the fifth flag dialect (`=== 'true'`, where `=1` was a silent no-op) is gone with it. | **PROOF (executed, mutation-proven):** `debug-routes.gate.spec.ts` gained the spellings that used to DEFEAT the gate — `APP_ENV=production/PROD/stage/STAGING`, `NODE_ENV=PRODUCTION`, `CRAVE_ENV=prod` — each asserted OFF even with `ENABLE_DEBUG_ROUTES=true`, plus the local opt-in spelled `true/TRUE/1/yes/on`. RED PROOF: restoring the literal comparisons turns exactly those 10 cases RED (`✕ is OFF for the deployed-env spelling production` … `✕ accepts the local opt-in spelled 1`) while the pre-existing cases stay green. Restored → 37/37 green.

F403 | api-core | apps/api/src/config/configuration.ts:49-58 vs apps/api/src/shared/config/app-env.ts:60-68 | `resolveAppEnv()` is implemented TWICE, byte-equivalently, including the same six-line comment about staging naming itself. `configuration.ts` already imports `normalizeAppEnv` and `isProdEnv` from `app-env.ts` — it re-derives the one function it did not import. `app-env.ts`'s header states the file exists because "five services hand-rolled exactly this"; the config assembly is the sixth. | LADDER: compensates for nothing; it is the drift the shared module was created to end, committed in the same session as the module. Bedrock: one fact, one declaration. Rederive: delete the local `resolveAppEnv` and `isProductionAppEnv` from configuration.ts; import `resolveAppEnv` and use `isProdEnv` directly. Net −12 lines, zero behaviour change. Red team: none — the two implementations are provably identical today, which is exactly the window in which deduplication is free. | FIXED (D33) — the local `resolveAppEnv`/`isProductionAppEnv` are deleted from configuration.ts; it imports `resolveAppEnv` and uses `isProdEnv` directly. Zero behaviour change (the shared version additionally trims, a superset). | `grep -rn "function resolveAppEnv" apps/api/src` now returns ONE hit (shared/config/app-env.ts); `job-control.ts`'s third copy is F420's, still open. tsc + build + full suite green.

F404 | api-core | apps/api/src/main.ts:114 + src/shared/filters/global-exception.filter.ts:41 + src/shared/logging/winston-logger.service.ts:23 + src/config/configuration.ts:12,33,122,139 + src/config/database-validation.service.ts:269 | **THE BOOT STORY HAS TWO ENVIRONMENT VOCABULARIES AND THE SECURITY-BEARING DECISIONS ALL USE THE WRONG ONE.** `app-env.ts` establishes `AppEnv` with two predicates precisely so staging can be deployed-but-not-prod. Boot then ignores it: `main.ts` computes `isProd = configService.get('NODE_ENV') === 'production'` and hangs helmet CSP strictness, HSTS, CORS, the global validation pipe's production mode, AND Swagger exposure off it; `GlobalExceptionFilter` independently recomputes the same expression to decide whether to leak error details; `winston-logger` and `configuration.ts` switch on NODE_ENV in five more places. Consequences that are already live: (1) the Swagger gate's own comment says "Dev/staging only" but staging runs `NODE_ENV=production`, so staging has NO docs — the comment describes the opposite of the code; (2) `getDatabasePoolSize()`'s `case 'staging': return '25'` is UNREACHABLE, because staging is spelled in `APP_ENV`, never `NODE_ENV` (it silently gets prod's 50); (3) `sentry.environment` falls back to `NODE_ENV`, so staging reports as `production` in Sentry unless `SENTRY_ENVIRONMENT` is set by hand — the exact indistinguishability `app-env.ts` was written to end; (4) `cloudinary.envPrefix` defaults to `'dev'`, so a deployed env missing `CLOUDINARY_ENV_PREFIX` writes assets into the dev namespace. | LADDER: NODE_ENV predates `AppEnv` and is load-bearing for tooling (`yarn install --production`, Nest, jest), so it cannot simply be renamed. Bedrock: NODE_ENV is a BUILD/TOOLCHAIN fact ("is this an optimized install"); `AppEnv` is a DEPLOYMENT fact ("whose money and whose users"). Every decision about exposure, secrets, sampling and namespaces is the second kind. Rederive: `main.ts` resolves `const appEnv = resolveAppEnv()` once and passes it down; helmet/HSTS/CORS/validation/Swagger key off `isDeployedEnv(appEnv)`; error-detail redaction keys off `isDeployedEnv` too (staging should not leak internals either); `sentry.environment` defaults to `appEnv`; `cloudinary.envPrefix` defaults to `appEnv`; `getDatabasePoolSize` takes `AppEnv` so its staging arm becomes reachable; NODE_ENV survives only where a toolchain reads it. Red team: the one real behaviour change is staging losing Swagger and gaining redacted errors — arguably desirable and cheap to opt back in with an explicit flag; the alternative (leaving it) keeps a documented lie in the file. | ESCALATED (design proposal; owner may want Swagger ON for staging, which is a one-line explicit choice under the new shape) | read + `grep -rn NODE_ENV apps/api/src` (16 non-spec sites); Swagger comment at main.ts:163 vs gate at :165.

F405 | api-core | apps/api/src/config/configuration.ts — namespaces `logging` (91-93), `sentry` (136-151), `throttler` (152-168), `jwt` (418-421), `onDemand` (424-428), `restaurantEnrichment` (446-450), `pushshift` (461-557), `clerk.publishableKey` (171) | **~160 OF THE CONFIG ASSEMBLY'S 559 LINES HAVE NO READER.** A census of every `config.get('<key>')` in `apps/api/src` (69 distinct keys) against every namespace declared here: `logging`, `sentry`, `throttler`, `jwt`, `onDemand`, `restaurantEnrichment` and `clerk.publishableKey` have **zero** readers; `pushshift` has exactly two (`targetSubreddits`, `fileTypes`) out of ~40 keys, so its `batchProcessing`, `checkpoints`, `storage.s3` and `validation` blocks (~95 lines) are entirely unread. The corresponding env vars are still set in `.env`: `JWT_SECRET` and `JWT_EXPIRATION` (a **dead secret** — auth is Clerk's; `grep -rn JWT_SECRET apps/api/src` returns nothing), all six `THROTTLER_*`, and `LOG_LEVEL` (which IS read, but by `winston.config.ts:99` reading `process.env` directly, not through this key). `throttler.decorator.ts:24` states outright that the tiers "no longer track the THROTTLER*\*\_LIMIT env vars" — the config block outlived the system it configured. | LADDER: each block compensated for a subsystem that has since been deleted (JWT auth → Clerk), rewritten (throttler → per-route decorator tiers), or half-adopted (pushshift → the archive ingestion path reads its own settings). Nothing removed the declaration, so `configuration.ts` now reads as a promise the code does not keep — and the province of "every default must be a fact, an owner choice, or a derivation" cannot be audited when a third of the defaults govern nothing. Bedrock: configuration is a contract between the operator and the code; a key nothing reads is not a lenient default, it is a lie about what the operator controls. Rederive: delete the seven zero-reader namespaces and the dead `pushshift` sub-blocks; fold `logging.level` into `winston.config.ts` (or make winston read the config key — one or the other, not both); delete `JWT_SECRET`/`JWT_EXPIRATION`/`THROTTLER*\_`from`.env`and from Railway. Add a boot-time or spec-time assertion that every declared leaf key is read somewhere, so the next dead block is a red test rather than a 2026 archaeology exercise. Red team: the deletion is only safe if no reader exists outside`apps/api/src`— verified:`apps/api/scripts`reads config only via`configuration()`for`llm`/`cloudinary`/`googlePlaces`, and no other workspace imports it. `SENTRY*DSN`/`SENTRY_ENVIRONMENT`/`SENTRY_RELEASE`stay live env vars (main.ts reads`process.env`for them) — it is the config MIRROR of them that is dead (see F406). | FIXED (D33) — deleted, each re-verified zero-reader first: `logging`, `sentry`, `jwt`, `onDemand`, `restaurantEnrichment`, `clerk.publishableKey`, and the dead `pushshift`sub-blocks (batchProcessing, checkpoints, storage.s3, validation, batchSize, processingTimeout). 133 lines removed. JWT_SECRET/JWT_EXPIRATION config deleted — the env vars are an ops note (dead secret, auth is Clerk's). **STOP-IF-WRONG DEVIATION, the important one:`throttler`was NOT deleted.** The finding (pass 1, 2026-08-02) recorded it as zero-reader, but D23 landed the next day and rewired it:`readThrottlerWindow` builds the key as a TEMPLATE LITERAL (`throttler.${window}.${field}`) and THROWS at boot if a value is missing or malformed, so a literal `get('throttler.` grep finds nothing while the block is live and load-bearing. Deleting it would have failed boot — exactly the dynamic-`config.get` form the banking law names. The block now carries a comment saying so. | **PROOF (executed):** a newline-tolerant census (`get<...>(`generics and quoted paths routinely span lines — the naive`<[^>]*>`regex initially HID`googlePlaces.operationLimits`, `pushshift.baseDirectory`and`pushshift.storage.local.\_`, all of which ARE read) yields 108 distinct keys across apps/api; none begins `logging.`/`sentry.`/`jwt.`/`onDemand.`/`restaurantEnrichment.`, and clerk reads 4 keys, not publishableKey. `configuration-readers.spec.ts`is the standing assertion F405 asked for: it flattens the assembled config to leaves and fails on any leaf with no reader, resolving template-literal readers too. MUTATION-PROVEN: re-adding`jwt: { secret: … }`turns it RED naming`jwt.secret`. NEW FINDINGS THE SPEC ITSELF PRODUCED (quarantined, NOT deleted — outside the ratified list): `reddit.requestsPerMinute`(whose env var is even published in REDDIT_API_INTEGRATION.md) and`tomtom.apiVersion` are zero-reader too; the spec asserts the orphan set EXACTLY, so they are visible and a NEW dead key still goes red.

F406 | api-core | apps/api/src/main.ts:15-30,34 vs src/config/configuration.ts:136-151 | **THE SENTRY SAMPLING POLICY IS WRITTEN TWICE, THE TWO COPIES DISAGREE, AND THE THOUGHTFUL ONE IS THE DEAD ONE.** `configuration.ts` computes `tracesSampleRate`/`profilesSampleRate` from **appEnv** with a comment explaining the choice ("full sampling in dev, 10% in prod (cost)"); `main.ts` computes the same two rates from **NODE_ENV**, before Nest exists. Only main.ts's copy runs — `Sentry.init()` must precede the module graph, and nothing reads `config.sentry.*` (F405). So the appEnv-awareness the config author wrote is inert, and staging (NODE_ENV=production) silently samples at 10% under the `production` environment tag. | LADDER: compensates for a genuine ordering constraint — Sentry MUST initialize before the app, so it cannot use `ConfigService`. The mistake was mirroring the values into config anyway instead of extracting them. Bedrock: an ordering constraint means the values need a home that is importable before Nest boots, not a second copy inside Nest. Rederive: one `src/sentry/sentry-init.ts` exporting `resolveSentryOptions(env: NodeJS.ProcessEnv)` built on `resolveAppEnv`/`isProdEnv`; `main.ts` calls it; the `sentry:` block leaves `configuration.ts` with the rest of F405. It is then unit-testable, which today it is not. Red team: staging's sampling and environment tag change (to `staging` at full rate) — the correct outcome, and the whole point of the staging split. | ESCALATED (design proposal; sequence with F404/F405) | read both; zero readers of `sentry.*` proven in F405.

F407 | api-core | apps/api/src/shared/logging/logging.interceptor.ts:150-178 vs src/shared/filters/global-exception.filter.ts:186-222 | **EVERY FAILED REQUEST IS LOGGED TWICE, AND THE INTERCEPTOR'S COPY IGNORES THE BY-STATUS LAW THE FILTER ENFORCES.** `LoggingInterceptor`'s `error` subscriber calls `this.logger.error(...)` for ANY exception, then the same exception reaches `GlobalExceptionFilter`, which logs it again. The filter is deliberate about level — its comment reads "Level by STATUS, never by exception class ... A typed AppException carrying a 500 must not hide at warn" — and correctly downgrades 4xx to `warn`. The interceptor has no status check at all, so a plain 404 or a validation 400 is emitted at ERROR level. Today that only costs duplicate log lines; the moment F400 is repaired it means **every 404 becomes a Sentry event**, which is precisely the metered-quota burn the debug-routes gate (F402) was hardened to prevent. Also note the interceptor reads `response.statusCode` in the error path, which on Fastify is still 200 at that point — the logged `statusCode` on failures is usually wrong. | LADDER: the interceptor's error branch compensates for the filter not existing yet, or for a fear that the filter might not catch everything. Bedrock: there is exactly one place that knows an exception's HTTP status, and it is the exception filter; an interceptor that fires before status resolution cannot classify severity and should not try. Rederive: delete `logError` from the interceptor and its `error` subscriber's logging (keep the `subscriber.error(error)` propagation); the filter remains the sole error logger, already correctly levelled and already carrying correlationId/userId. Red team: the only loss is a log line for an exception that somehow bypasses the global filter — impossible for HTTP, and the filter is `@Catch()` unqualified. **This must land BEFORE or WITH F400**, or repairing crash reporting floods Sentry with 4xx. | FIXED (D33, landed FIRST) — `logError` and the error-subscriber's logging are deleted from `LoggingInterceptor`; `subscriber.error(error)` propagation is unchanged. `GlobalExceptionFilter` is the sole error logger, already levelled by STATUS and already carrying correlationId + the VERIFIED userId. The interceptor now carries the reason, including why it cannot classify severity (on Fastify `response.statusCode` is still 200 in the error path). | **PROOF (executed, mutation-proven):** new `logging.interceptor.spec.ts` — a thrown exception is logged at NO level (neither error nor warn), the exception still propagates unchanged, and successful responses still log at http. RED PROOF: re-adding a `this.logger.error(...)` to the error subscriber turns it RED ('Expected number of calls: 0, Received number of calls: 1'). Restored → green.

F408 | api-core | apps/api/src/shared/logging/correlation.utils.ts:113-141, used at logging.interceptor.ts:38-44 | **THE `userId` IN EVERY REQUEST LOG IS ATTACKER-CONTROLLED.** `extractUserIdFromToken` base64-decodes the JWT payload segment and returns `sub`/`userId` **with no signature verification** — its own comment says "In production, you'd use proper JWT verification". The `LoggingInterceptor` calls it on every request and stamps the result into the AsyncLocalStorage context, which flows into every log line for that request and into `GlobalExceptionFilter`'s log context. Anyone can send `Authorization: Bearer x.<base64 of {"sub":"user_someoneelse"}>.x` and author the `userId` field of the server's own logs — audit-trail forgery, and (post-F400) Sentry `extra` too. The real, verified user id already exists on `request.user.userId` after `ClerkAuthGuard`, and `SentryInterceptor` correctly uses that one. | LADDER: compensates for the logging interceptor running BEFORE guards, so no verified user exists yet at that point. Bedrock: an unverified claim is not an identity; the correct value for "who is this" before authentication runs is _unknown_. Rederive: delete `extractUserIdFromToken` (69 lines incl. its `manual base64` path) and leave `context.userId` undefined at request-log time; populate it from `request.user.userId` in the RESPONSE log and in the filter's `logContext` (the filter already reads `request.user.userId` at :204 — the verified one). Correlation across the request is already carried by `correlationId`, which is what it is for. Red team: incoming request lines lose a userId they were never entitled to assert; every line that matters (response, error) gains a verified one. | ESCALATED (design proposal; security-relevant) | read; `correlation.utils.ts:127` `Buffer.from(token.split('.')[1],'base64')` with no verify step, called unconditionally at `logging.interceptor.ts:38`.

F409 | api-core | apps/api/.eslintrc.json | **A DEAD CONFIG FILE THAT EXTENDS A FILE THAT DOES NOT EXIST — AND WHOSE RULES PEOPLE BELIEVE ARE IN FORCE.** It declares `"extends": ["../../.eslintrc.json"]`; there is no `.eslintrc.json` at the repo root. The live config is `apps/api/eslint.config.mjs` (flat config, ESLint 9), which is 10KB and was modified today. The dead file's second override block relaxes ten `@typescript-eslint/no-unsafe-*` rules to `warn` for `*.spec.ts`, `**/test/**`, `**/*script*.ts` — a relaxation a reader would reasonably assume governs the 100 files in `scripts/`. It governs nothing. | LADDER: leftover from the pre-flat-config era; ESLint 9 ignores `.eslintrc*` unless `ESLINT_USE_FLAT_CONFIG=false`, so it rotted silently rather than erroring. Bedrock: a config file that cannot fail is indistinguishable from a comment, and this one makes a claim about lint policy that is false. Rederive: delete `apps/api/.eslintrc.json`. If the script/spec relaxations are actually wanted, express them as an override block in `eslint.config.mjs` where they will take effect. Red team: banking hunt — nothing references it (`grep -rn "eslintrc" --exclude-dir=node_modules` finds only the file itself); `npx eslint` runs clean with the missing `extends`, proving non-consumption. | FIXED (D33) — `apps/api/.eslintrc.json` DELETED. | **PROOF (re-verified at implement time):** the root `.eslintrc.json` it extends still does not exist (root has `.eslintrc.js`, which only apps/mobile extends); `grep -rIn eslintrc` across the repo finds no consumer of the api file; `npx eslint src/shared/config/env-flag.ts` exits 0 both before AND after deletion, and `--print-config` returns the flat `eslint.config.mjs` rule set. Post-deletion `npx eslint src scripts` runs clean of any config error.

F410 | api-core | apps/api/package.json `jest.rootDir: "src"` + `test:e2e` + apps/api/test/_.ts | **THE TEST HARNESS SCANS ONLY `src/`, AND TWO OF ITS THREE MOVING PARTS ARE INERT.** (a) `rootDir: "src"` means a `_.spec.ts`under`apps/api/scripts/`or`apps/api/test/`would never be discovered — the 100-file scripts territory is structurally untestable without noticing. (b)`test:e2e`runs`jest --config ./test/jest-e2e.json`; that file does not exist, so the script fails instantly. (c) `test/jest.setup.ts`, `test/jest.global-setup.ts`, `test/jest.global-teardown.ts`are wired into`setupFilesAfterEach`/`globalSetup`/`globalTeardown`and contain, respectively,`export {}`and two empty async functions — ceremony that costs three module loads per run and pays nothing. (d)`test:db`passes`--testPathIgnorePatterns=[]`, which jest reads as the literal regex `[]`, not an empty list. | LADDER: scaffolding from `nest new`that was never reconciled with how this codebase actually tests (co-located specs under`src/`). Bedrock: a harness should make the untested places visible, not silently exclude them; a script that cannot run is worse than an absent one because it implies a capability. Rederive: `rootDir`→`.`with`testRegex`unchanged (co-located specs still match;`scripts/`becomes testable, which F411 wants); delete`test:e2e`until an e2e config exists; delete the three placeholder files and their three jest keys; fix or delete`test:db`. Red team: widening `rootDir`could pick up specs in`dist/`— add`testPathIgnorePatterns: ["/dist/", "/node_modules/"]`, which is standard. | ESCALATED (design proposal) | read; `ls apps/api/test/`→ exactly the three placeholders, no`jest-e2e.json`; `find apps/api/scripts -name '\*.spec.ts'` → none (nothing to lose today, everything to lose tomorrow).

F411 | api-scripts | apps/api/scripts/archive/batch-slice-test.ts:100 vs src/shared/utils/stop-crons.ts | **THE CRON-CONTAINMENT GUARANTEE IS A CONVENTION HELD BY 45 FILES, AND ONE FILE HAS ALREADY ESCAPED IT.** `stop-crons.ts`'s header states the hazard precisely: every `NestFactory.createApplicationContext(AppModule)` registers all ~20 `@Cron` jobs and starts them ticking (`PROCESS_ROLE` defaults to `'all'`, so `isSchedulerRuntime()` is true), which "double-drains governed queues and double-spends vendor pools". The remedy is `stopCronsForScript(app)` called by hand. 45 of 46 scripts call it; `scripts/archive/batch-slice-test.ts` boots the full `AppModule` and does not. There is no spec enforcing it. | LADDER: the helper compensates for scripts sharing the app's module graph. But the helper only closes the hazard for whoever remembers to call it — and the codebase has already invented the structural fix for exactly this shape: `src/shared/testing/import-scan.ts`'s `filesImporting()` allow-list, written after two guards were outflanked by "the files I happened to think of". That tool is one directory away and has never been pointed at this. Bedrock: a containment property must be a property of the boot, not of the author's memory. Rederive (either, prefer the first): (1) a `bootScriptContext()` helper in `stop-crons.ts` that does `createApplicationContext` + `stopCronsForScript` as one call, and a lockdown spec asserting `NestFactory.createApplicationContext` appears in NO file under `scripts/` except that helper — the `codeOnly()` + `filesImporting()` pattern already built for this; or (2) at minimum a spec that walks `scripts/**` and fails on any file mentioning `createApplicationContext` without `stopCronsForScript` (needs F410's `rootDir` fix to run). Red team: (1) makes the escape unrepresentable and deletes 45 duplicated call pairs; the new failure is a script that legitimately wants crons, which does not exist and would be loud. | FIXED (D33) — `scripts/archive/batch-slice-test.ts` now calls `stopCronsForScript(app)` (46 of 46), and containment stopped being a convention: `src/shared/testing/script-containment.spec.ts` walks the WHOLE scripts tree and fails on any file that boots a Nest context without stopping the crons. It reads sources through `codeOnly()` — the precedent breach was a guard satisfied by a COMMENT sixteen lines above the missing call — and carries a non-empty-sweep assertion so it can never go green by losing the tree. The heavier option (a `bootScriptContext()` helper replacing 45 call pairs) was NOT taken: the ratified scope was the lockdown, and the spec makes the escape detectable without a 45-file rewrite. | **PROOF (executed, RED BEFORE GREEN, in that order):** the spec was written and run BEFORE the fix and failed naming exactly `archive/batch-slice-test.ts` (`Received: Array [ "archive/batch-slice-test.ts" ]`) — 1 of 46, matching the finding. The script was then contained and the suite went green, 4/4.

F412 | api-core | apps/api/src/shared/utils/stop-crons.ts:41-63 + src/main.ts:190 | `stopCronsUnlessWorker(app)` is an **empty function with an eslint-disable for its unused parameter**, honestly documented as "a documented NO-OP by construction" since the Railway cutover (the module gate in `app.module.ts` is the real guarantee, and probing `app.get(SchedulerRegistry)` on the application proxy tears the process down). But `main.ts:189` still introduces the call as "ONE chokepoint for 'which process runs scheduled work'", which is now false — the chokepoint is `app.module.ts:50`'s `...(runtimeWithSchedulers ? [ScheduleModule.forRoot()] : [])`. A future reader adding cron logic will add it to the no-op. | LADDER: kept deliberately as "the bootstrap seam" for a future need. Bedrock: an empty function is not a seam, it is a place a change can hide; the seam is wherever the decision is actually made. Rederive: delete `stopCronsUnlessWorker` and its call; move its excellent explanation (why probing the proxy is fatal, why the module gate is the guarantee) into the comment beside the `ScheduleModule` gate in `app.module.ts`, where the decision lives. `stopCronsForScript` stays — it is real. Red team: nothing executes today, so deletion is behaviour-identical; the risk is losing the hard-won explanation, which the move preserves. | FIXED (D33) — `stopCronsUnlessWorker` and its main.ts call are deleted (the function body was one comment block and no statements). main.ts's false 'ONE chokepoint' sentence is gone. Its hard-won explanation — why a non-worker runtime has no SchedulerRegistry, and why probing `app.get(SchedulerRegistry)` on the application PROXY tears the process down before any try/catch sees it — MOVED to app.module.ts beside the `ScheduleModule.forRoot()` gate, which is where the decision actually lives, with a 'do not restore a runtime probe here' warning. `stopCronsForScript` stays; it is real. | Behaviour-identical by construction (nothing executed). tsc + build + full suite green; boot verified on :3399 with PROCESS_ROLE=api.

F413 | api-core | apps/api/tsconfig.json:35 | `"include"` lists `"onboard-subreddit.ts"` at the api ROOT; no such file exists there (the real one is `scripts/onboard-subreddit.ts`, already covered by the `scripts/**/*` glob two lines up). A stale entry that also happens to be the only thing making `onboard-subreddit.ts` look "wired" in a reference census. | LADDER: a leftover from when the script lived at the package root. Bedrock: an include pattern that matches nothing is noise that misleads the next reader. Rederive: delete the line. Red team: `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` is green today and stays green — the file is already compiled via `scripts/**/*`. | FIXED (D33) — the stale `"onboard-subreddit.ts"` include is deleted from apps/api/tsconfig.json. | `ls apps/api/onboard-subreddit.ts` → no such file; the real script is covered by the `scripts/**/*` glob two lines up. `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` exits 0 after the deletion.

F414 | api-scripts | apps/api/scripts/ (100 files) | **THE SCRIPTS DIRECTORY HAS NO LIFECYCLE — AND THAT, NOT ROT, IS THE DEFECT.** Full census: `tsc --noEmit` compiles all of it clean, and spot-executed orphans still work (see proof), so nothing here is broken. What is missing is any rule for entry or exit. Of 100 files: **26 are executably wired** (5 via `apps/api/package.json` scripts; 11 via `scripts/rig/reextract.sh` + `cost-reconcile.sh`; 1 via `.claude/skills/reextract/SKILL.md`; 2 via `eslint.config.mjs` allow-lists; the rest imported/read by another script); **~52 are mentioned only in a `plans/` or `product/` doc or a source comment** — banked as provenance, not runnable by anything; and **22 have zero references anywhere outside `audit/COVERAGE.md`**, including four `rt-*.ts` files committed TODAY. No `.github/workflows/` file references any script in this directory. Two READMEs (`scripts/data-fixes/README.md`, `scripts/search-harness/README.md`) are linked from nothing. `scripts/lib/gazetteer-names.ts` has no importer at all — its banking note points at `seed-us-places`, a script that no longer exists. | LADDER: compensates for a genuinely useful practice — writing a probe to answer one question during an investigation — colliding with the absence of any convention for what happens to the probe afterward. The `tsconfig.json` exclusion of `scripts/search-harness/rt-*.ts` ("red-team/exploratory harnesses: throwaway by convention") shows the convention was already _felt_; it just was not made operational, and it stops at one glob. Bedrock: a script is one of exactly three things and should say which in its own header — (a) **operational tooling** invoked by a runner (package.json / rig / skill), (b) **a banked probe** whose value is the recorded RESULT, kept so the finding is reproducible, or (c) **scratch**, valuable for a day. Today all three are one undifferentiated pile, so the only way to know is a repo-wide grep — which is why the census had to be run at all. Rederive: adopt the `rt-*` prefix convention the tsconfig already half-encodes, generalized: a mandatory one-line header on every script naming its class and its runner or its banked finding; `scripts/scratch/` (gitignored) for class (c); a lockdown spec that fails on a script with no class header. Then a deletion sweep is mechanical instead of a judgement call per file. Red team: the 22 zero-reference scripts are NOT proposed for deletion — several are working, valuable diagnostics (`gazetteer-probe`, `demand-law-proof`, `matcher-probe`) whose only sin is that nobody wrote down what they are for; deleting working instruments to satisfy a census is the wrong direction. The proposal is to make the class explicit and let the sweep follow. Two safe deletions stand alone: `scripts/lib/gazetteer-names.ts` (zero importers, its stated consumer deleted) and the two unlinked READMEs should be linked from `product/launch-runbook.md` / `plans/` rather than deleted. | FIXED (D33), scope-bounded — the class convention is now operational and enforced, and NO script was mass-deleted (the finding's own red team forbids it). Every script that boots a Nest context (46 files) carries a `@script-class:` header naming one of `operational` / `probe` / `scratch`; `script-containment.spec.ts` fails on an undeclared one and on a class outside the three. Classes were DERIVED, not guessed: `operational` iff a real reference census over package.json, scripts/rig/\*.sh, .claude/, .github/ and eslint.config.mjs finds a runner, and the header names that runner (7 files). The other 39 are `probe` and their headers say honestly that the finding is NOT YET BANKED and that the file is a deletion candidate until someone writes it down — inventing a finding would have been worse than leaving the gap visible. | **PROOF (executed):** 46/46 stamped, derived from the executed census (`/tmp/runners.json`); `npx jest src/shared/testing/script-containment` 4/4 green; `tsc --noEmit -p apps/api/tsconfig.json` exit 0 over all of scripts/\*\*; `npx eslint src scripts` shows no new error. NOT DONE (still open, out of ratified scope): the two safe stand-alone deletions the finding names (`scripts/lib/gazetteer-names.ts`, zero importers with its stated consumer deleted) and linking the two orphan READMEs.

F415 | api-core | apps/api/src/shared/logging/logger.service.ts:16-31 vs logger.interface.ts:4-21, and logger.service.ts:279-345 vs winston-logger.service.ts:190-262 | Riding on F400: the orphan logger duplicates `LogMetadata` (21 lines, byte-identical field-for-field) and both `sanitizeMetadata`/`sanitizeNestedObject` (~70 lines, near-identical — `winston-logger`'s version additionally strips `cookie`/`session` from nested objects and deletes null leaves, so the two have already drifted). Deleting the orphan per F400 removes all of it. | LADDER: none — pure supersession residue. Bedrock: one declaration per fact. Rederive: covered by F400's deletion. | FIXED — subsumed by F400's deletion of `logging/logger.service.ts`: the duplicate 21-line `LogMetadata` and both drifted sanitizer copies went with it. | `grep -rn "logging/logger.service" apps/api/src` → zero hits. Build + full suite green.

F416 | api-core | apps/api/src/shared/logging/winston-logger.service.ts:200-215 + logging.interceptor.ts:190-232 | **THE LOG REDACTION IS EXACT-KEY-MATCH, AND REQUEST BODIES ARE LOGGED WHOLESALE.** `sanitizeMetadata` redacts a key only when it equals one of `password/token/secret/key/authorization/cookie/session` exactly — so `apiKey`, `accessToken`, `refreshToken`, `clientSecret`, `webhookSecret`, `sessionId` all pass through verbatim, and the codebase uses those spellings throughout its config. Separately, `LoggingInterceptor.logRequest` logs the **entire body of every non-GET request under 10KB**, sanitized by the same exact-match rule applied only at the TOP level (`sanitizeBody` does not recurse), so a nested credential or any user-authored content (poll comments, messages, photo captions, list names) lands in the log stream — and, post-F400, in Sentry `extra`. `sanitizeBody` does redact `email`, which shows PII was considered; the mechanism just cannot reach one level down. | LADDER: compensates for not knowing which fields are sensitive, by naming the ones someone thought of. Bedrock: redaction by denylist fails open by construction — every new field name is a leak until someone remembers it. Rederive: (a) match keys case-insensitively on a SUBSTRING set (`key`, `token`, `secret`, `password`, `auth`, `cookie`, `session`) so `apiKey`/`accessToken` are caught, and recurse in `sanitizeBody` as `sanitizeNestedObject` already does; (b) better, stop logging request bodies by default — gate it behind a dev-only flag, since the correlationId + route + status already answer every question the body was there for. Red team: (a) is a strictly-larger redaction set, so the only risk is over-redacting a field named e.g. `keyword` — acceptable for a log; (b) removes a debugging affordance that is only reachable in dev anyway once the flag exists. | ESCALATED (design proposal; sequence with F400) | read; `sensitiveFields.forEach(f => { if (f in merged) ... })` is exact-key `in`, and `sanitizeBody` has no recursive branch.

F417 | api-scripts | apps/api/scripts/reload/\*.sql (6 files) | **NOT A DEFECT — VERIFIED LIVE AND SCHEMA-COHERENT BY EXECUTION.** All six files of the re-extract choreography were run against the local mirror (`crave_search`, 17,357 entities, 89,903 source documents across `austinfood`/`foodnyc`) and every one executes cleanly against the current schema, with `preserved-anchors.sql` correctly included via `\ir` by the other three consumers. The suite is the strongest-written code in this territory: each destructive statement carries the incident that produced it, the preservation laws (RESTAURANT LAW, USER-ANCHOR LAW, REFERENCED-MEANS-ALIVE, TOMBSTONES-ARE-MEMORY) live in one file rather than being restated per script, and `shadow-discard.sql` opens with a `DO` block that REFUSES to discard an activated generation. IDEAL-VERIFIED. One observation, not a defect: `wipe-city-derived.sql` ends in `COMMIT`, so it cannot be dry-run as written — a `-v dryrun=1` branch mirroring `gc-unsupported-entities.sql`'s `\if :{?execute}` pattern would let the operator rehearse the exact file they are about to run instead of a hand-edited copy (which is what this audit had to do). | LADDER: n/a. | IDEAL-VERIFIED (one small proposal: dry-run parity with the gc script) | **PROOF (all executed, all rolled back):** `anchor-audit.sql -v since='2026-07-30 20:00'` → twins + starved sections + 200 semantic-twin candidates, `ROLLBACK`. `gc-unsupported-entities.sql` dry run → `gc_candidates 17` (7 food, 10 restaurant), `ROLLBACK`. `shadow-diff.sql -v communities=austinfood -v prompt_hash=<live hash>` → coverage + lost-support + new-under-shadow sections, `ROLLBACK`. `wipe-city-derived.sql -v communities=austinfood` with the trailing `COMMIT` rewritten to `ROLLBACK` → `target_docs 39804 | affected_restaurants 2915 | preserved_entities 5740 | preserved_connections 5636 | FIXED (D33) — `wipe-city-derived.sql`gained the dry-run branch its gc sibling has: the audit SELECT is computed either way, then`\if :{?dryrun}`ROLLBACKs with an echo,`\else`COMMITs. Default is COMMIT deliberately (opt-IN rehearsal), so every existing invocation still means what it meant — the mirror image of gc-unsupported-entities.sql's opt-IN destruction, and the comment says why: gc is a routine sweep, this is the disaster tool. | **PROOF (executed against the local mirror):**`psql crave_search -v communities='austinfood' -v dryrun=1 -f wipe-city-derived.sql`→`target_docs 39804 | affected_restaurants 2915 | preserved_entities 5740 | preserved_connections 5636 | entities_deleted 6406 | entities_remaining 10951`, then `DRY RUN — nothing written.`and`ROLLBACK`. The counts match this finding's original hand-edited-copy rehearsal EXACTLY, which is the point: the operator can now rehearse the file they are about to run. `select count(\*) from core_entities` → 17357, unchanged.

F418 | api-core | apps/api/pnpm-lock.yaml (12,865 lines / 340KB, lockfileVersion 9, mtime Apr 17) | **A SECOND, STALE DEPENDENCY TRUTH FOR A PACKAGE MANAGER THIS REPO DOES NOT USE.** The workspace is yarn: root `yarn.lock` (608KB, touched 2026-07-25), `packageManager`/corepack pinned to `yarn@1.22.22`, and the Dockerfile runs `yarn install --frozen-lockfile` twice. There is no root `pnpm-lock.yaml` and no `apps/api/yarn.lock` — this file is the only pnpm artifact in the repo, four months stale, and it sits in the api workspace where a `pnpm install` would resolve an entirely different dependency tree from the one that has been tested and deployed. | LADDER: residue from an early package-manager evaluation. Bedrock: a lockfile is an assertion about exactly what will be installed; two lockfiles for one package are two contradictory assertions, and the resolution is not "whichever tool you happen to run". Rederive: delete it. Red team: banking hunt — `grep -rn "pnpm" --exclude-dir=node_modules` finds it referenced by no script, no CI workflow, no Dockerfile, no doc; the only other `pnpm` strings in the repo are `pnpm-debug.log*` ignore lines. Deleting it removes a way to silently build against untested versions. | FIXED (D33) — `apps/api/pnpm-lock.yaml` DELETED (340KB, four months stale, the repo's only pnpm artifact). | **PROOF (re-verified at implement time):** repo-wide `grep -rIn pnpm` finds it referenced by no script, workflow, Dockerfile or doc (the only other pnpm strings are `pnpm-debug.log*` ignore lines and an unrelated plan sentence); root `yarn.lock` present, `apps/api/Dockerfile` runs `yarn install --frozen-lockfile`. Build + full suite green after deletion.

F419 | api-core | apps/api/.dockerignore + apps/api/.jestignore | **TWO IGNORE FILES THAT NOTHING READS, ONE OF WHICH WOULD BREAK THE BUILD IF IT EVER DID.** (a) `apps/api/.dockerignore` is never consulted: Docker reads `.dockerignore` from the BUILD CONTEXT root, and `scripts/rig/deploy.sh` encodes "ALWAYS deploys from the repo ROOT (`railway up` uploads the cwd as the build context)" with `railway.json` pointing `dockerfilePath` at `apps/api/Dockerfile`. The root `.dockerignore` is the live one. Worse, if the context ever moved to `apps/api`, this file's `*.md` rule would strip `modules/external-integrations/llm/prompts/*.md` — which `nest-cli.json` declares as build ASSETS, and which CLAUDE.md records as the cause of a real bootstrap crash ("ENOENT relevance-gate-prompt.md"). A dormant landmine, not merely dead. (b) `.jestignore` is not a Jest feature at all — Jest has no `.jestignore`; exclusion is `testPathIgnorePatterns` in `package.json`, which is where the real config lives. Its own header ("Ignore files that cause Jest compilation warnings") describes a job it has never done. | LADDER: both compensate for a belief about how a tool is configured, held without checking. Bedrock: a config file's existence proves nothing; only the tool reading it does. Rederive: delete both. If per-workspace docker exclusions are ever wanted, they belong in the root `.dockerignore` under an `apps/api/` prefix, where they will take effect. Red team: deleting a file nothing reads cannot change behaviour; the risk is the opposite — leaving `.dockerignore` there invites someone to "fix" the build context and detonate it. | FIXED (D33) — BOTH deleted: `apps/api/.dockerignore` (never consulted — Docker reads `.dockerignore` from the BUILD CONTEXT root, and deploy.sh + railway.json put the context at the repo ROOT, where a live `.dockerignore` exists; the api copy was also a dormant landmine, since its `*.md` rule would strip the nest-cli prompt ASSETS that once caused a real ENOENT bootstrap crash) and `apps/api/.jestignore` (not a Jest feature at all — exclusion is `testPathIgnorePatterns`). | **PROOF (re-verified at implement time):** root `.dockerignore` present; `railway.json:5` `dockerfilePath: apps/api/Dockerfile` with `deploy.sh:16-18` recording the repo-ROOT context; no reference to either file anywhere in the repo. Full suite green after deletion (140 suites / 1260 tests).

F420 | api-core | apps/api/job-control.ts:10-30 | **A 357-LINE OPERATIONAL TOOL LIVING OUTSIDE EVERY CONVENTION THAT GOVERNS OPERATIONAL TOOLS, CARRYING A THIRD HAND-ROLLED `resolveAppEnv`.** It sits at the package ROOT rather than in `scripts/`, so it escapes the scripts census, the `stopCronsForScript` convention (it does not boot Nest, so no live hazard — but nothing would have caught it if it did), and — per `plans/rebuild-execution-ledger.md:1248` — it was consciously left "outside the lint project ... cosmetic only". It re-implements `resolveAppEnv()` and `resolveBullPrefix()` inline against raw `process.env`, making it the third copy of the first (with `configuration.ts`, F403) and the second of the second (with `configuration.ts:134`'s `bull.prefix`). Because it talks to Redis by BullMQ key prefix, a drift between its prefix derivation and the app's means it inspects and CLEARS the wrong environment's queues — and its own help text advertises a `clear` command. | LADDER: compensates for `scripts/` not existing (or not being the habit) when it was written, and for the operator wanting a zero-boot Redis tool. Bedrock: the queue namespace is one fact owned by the app's configuration; any tool that mutates those queues must derive the namespace the same way the app does, or it is operating on a namespace it guessed. Rederive: move it to `scripts/job-control.ts`, bring it into the lint project, and import `resolveAppEnv` from `shared/config/app-env.ts` plus a single exported `bullPrefixFor(appEnv)` that `configuration.ts` also calls — one derivation, two callers. Red team: the move is cosmetic; the import is the substance, and it makes a wrong-environment `clear` impossible-by-construction rather than correct-by-coincidence. | ESCALATED (design proposal) | read; `grep -rn "function resolveAppEnv"` → `shared/config/app-env.ts`, `config/configuration.ts`, `job-control.ts` (three copies); `plans/rebuild-execution-ledger.md:1248` records the deliberate exclusion.

F421 | api-core | apps/api/test/ + apps/api/src/shared/testing/ | **NOT A DEFECT — the two testing helpers are the best-derived code in this territory.** `code-only.ts` (strip comments before a source scanner reads them) and `import-scan.ts` (`filesImporting()` — an allow-list over the WHOLE tree) each carry the specific breaches that produced them: a guard satisfied by a COMMENT sixteen lines above the missing call, and two successive guards outflanked by files their authors did not think of. Both generalize a fix that had already been solved locally, which is the correct response to a repeat. IDEAL-VERIFIED. Recorded here because F411 identifies the next place `filesImporting()` should be pointed (script cron containment) and F400 the next place a scanner-style lockdown is needed (the logger→Sentry binding) — the tools exist; they are simply under-applied. | LADDER: n/a. | IDEAL-VERIFIED | read; both headers name the executed breach, not a hypothetical.

F422 | api-core | apps/api/src/shared/exceptions/app-exception.base.ts:9,23-28 + all 22 subclasses | **`isOperational` IS A DISCRIMINATOR THAT NEVER DISCRIMINATES — AND ITS ONE EFFECT IS LEAKING INTERNALS TO PROD CLIENTS.** `AppException` declares `abstract readonly isOperational: boolean`, and `getClientSafeMessage(isProd)` redacts the message only when `isProd && !this.isOperational`. A census of every subclass in `apps/api/src` finds **22 declarations, all `= true`, and zero `false`** — including `DatabaseException` (HTTP 500, message `Database create failed for User`), `ExternalServiceException` (502, `Gemini service error during generateContent`), and `DatabaseConfigurationError`. So the redaction branch is unreachable, and `GlobalExceptionFilter:75` returns those messages verbatim to production callers, while the very next branch of the same method carefully replaces a plain `Error` with `'An internal error occurred'`. A typed exception is currently LESS redacted than an untyped one. | LADDER: compensates for wanting to distinguish "an expected failure the client should hear about" from "a bug", encoded as a per-class boolean the author must remember to set correctly — and every author defaulted to `true` because `true` is the permissive, working-looking value. Bedrock: whether a message is safe to show a client is a property of the MESSAGE, not of the class; and the fact the filter actually needs — "is this a 5xx" — is already carried by the status, which is why `logError` was rewritten to level "by STATUS, never by exception class". Rederive: delete `isOperational` and make `getClientSafeMessage(isProd)` redact on `isProd && this.getStatus() >= 500`, matching the untyped path exactly. Exceptions that genuinely want a client-visible 5xx message (none today) would opt in explicitly with a `clientMessage` field, which is a positive assertion rather than a forgotten negative. Red team: prod stops returning DB/vendor internals — the intended behaviour all along; nothing else reads the flag except `getLogContext()`, where it is a constant. | FIXED (D33) — `isOperational` DELETED: the abstract declaration, all 22 `= true` subclass declarations, and the `getLogContext()` field where it was a constant. `getClientSafeMessage(isProd)` now redacts on `isProd && this.getStatus() >= 500`, matching the untyped-error path in the same filter exactly, and matching `logError`'s ratified 'by STATUS, never by exception class'. | **PROOF (executed, mutation-proven):** `client-safe-message.spec.ts` — the Database 5xx message no longer leaks (`'An internal error occurred'`, asserted NOT to contain 'Database'), 4xx still shows, non-prod still shows, the flag cannot return as a per-class opt-out, and a WHOLE-MODULE sweep over every AppException subclass asserts every constructible 5xx redacts (with a non-empty-sweep guard, since an empty loop is a green metric that cannot show red). RED PROOF: neutering the redaction branch turns 2 of the 5 RED, including the whole-module sweep. Census re-verified before deletion: 22 `= true`, zero `false`.

F423 | api-core | apps/api/src/shared/types/error-interfaces.ts:83-90, used at src/shared/filters/global-exception.filter.ts:110-112 | **THE TYPE GUARDS CANNOT SAY NO, AND ONE OF THEM MISCLASSIFIES NETWORK FAILURES AS DATABASE ERRORS IN EVERY HTTP RESPONSE.** `isPrismaError(error)` returns true for anything that is an object with a `code` and a `message` — which describes essentially every Node system error (`ECONNREFUSED`, `ENOTFOUND`, `ETIMEDOUT`), every Axios error, and most vendor SDK errors. `GlobalExceptionFilter.extractErrorDetails` tests it BEFORE the generic branch, so a Redis outage or a Gemini connection reset is reported to the caller as `errorCode: 'DATABASE_ERROR'`, message `'Database operation failed'`, and logged the same way — sending the on-call reader to the wrong subsystem. `isRedditApiError` is worse still (`'error' in error || 'message' in error` — true for every `Error`), and `isHttpError` claims any object with a message and a status. The file's own header calls itself a "Template for gradual migration ... Priority: Medium", yet it sits on the hot path of the global exception filter. | LADDER: compensates for `unknown`-typed catch blocks at a time when the alternative was `any`. Bedrock: a type guard's value is entirely in its ability to return FALSE; a structural test over two ubiquitous field names is not a discriminator, it is an assertion dressed as a check. Prisma ships the real answer — `error instanceof Prisma.PrismaClientKnownRequestError` (and `...UnknownRequestError`, `...ValidationError`), which is exact and already imported elsewhere in this codebase. Rederive: `isPrismaError` becomes the `instanceof` test; `isRedditApiError`/`isLLMApiError`/`isDatabaseConnectionError` are deleted unless a caller can state a field combination unique to that vendor (none currently can); `getErrorMessage`/`getErrorCode` stay — they are honest best-effort extractors and are the only parts with real callers. Red team: `instanceof` fails across duplicate `@prisma/client` copies in a monorepo — verify with a single boot probe, and fall back to `error.name.startsWith('PrismaClient')` if it does; either is vastly narrower than "has a code". The behaviour change is that non-Prisma failures stop being labelled DATABASE_ERROR, which is the whole point. | ESCALATED (design proposal) | read; `filters/global-exception.filter.ts:110` calls `isPrismaError(exception)` ahead of the unexpected-error branch, and `handlePrismaError`'s `default:` arm produces `DATABASE_ERROR` for any unrecognised `code`.

The class accrues ~2 observations/cycle. (b) OUTCOME = resultCount, documents returned per search, observed at the orchestrator chokepoint that already writes the harvest snapshot; per-term DOWNSTREAM yield (new mentions/entities) is unattributable BY CONSTRUCTION — enqueueKeywordBatches unions post ids across all of a cycle's terms before any processing — and was not fabricated. Harvests only; errors/denials are vendor facts, not measurements. (c) COLD START = all classes Infinity → optimistic tie → order by coverage rotation (never-attempted first, then oldest), a scan policy carrying no weight; act, then measure. EXPLORE_RECENT_ATTEMPT_DAYS deleted (both refs were novelty). **The gate also found what D41 fixed:** the registry had zero consumers and no durable side, so an in-memory belief died every deploy against a 30d half-life — new `estimator_state` table (migration 20260803140000, additive), registry gains hydrate() load-on-first-read + observeDurable() persist-on-observe; this is the registry-WIDE pattern. **PROOF:** keyword-explore-ucb.spec.ts, 7 specs — unobserved class wins optimistically; the measured 40-doc class outranks the measured 0-doc class in DOCUMENT units; coverage rotation orders within a class; the two wildly-different-proxy candidates now score IDENTICALLY; beliefs survive a process restart through the store while a store-less registry reads n=0/Infinity. ### Phase-3 implementation outcomes (content-processing, 2026-08-03)

Green gate: `yarn build` clean; `npx jest content-processing` = 22 suites / 164 tests pass.
NOTE: this territory was heavily mid-flight in a shared tree — several items were
ALREADY landed (committed or uncommitted) when Phase-3 opened; those are marked
(pre-landed, verified).

- F450 FIXED (pre-landed, verified) — entityDetails N+1 + its `entityDetails` type field already deleted; re-grepped zero consumers.
- F451 FIXED — `isRedditSubmission` now accepts numeric-string `created_utc` (its declared shape). Proof: archive-integrity.spec.ts "F451: isRedditSubmission accepts…" (RED: string fixture never survived pre-fix).
- F452 FIXED — normalizePostId/ensureCommentId are derive-or-refuse (return null → record DROPPED, counted at WARN); Math.random() ids gone. Proof: archive-integrity.spec.ts "DROPS an id-less submission — never a random id".
- F453 FIXED — zstd resolution GATED on clean process exit; a truncated .zst now rejects (readline 'close' no longer resolves before the non-zero exit). Proof: archive-integrity.spec.ts "rejects a truncated zstd frame".
- F454 FIXED (pre-landed, verified) — archive + keyword workers THROW (buildNoopResult / catch-returns-result arms gone); chronological throw is the template.
- F455 FIXED — ONE `resolveTestLimit(envVar, cap)` helper (reddit-collector/test-limit.ts) with isProdEnv refusal; wired into all three levers (TEST_CHRONO_MAX_POSTS, TEST_ARCHIVE_MAX_POSTS, TEST_REDDIT_FETCH_LIMIT). Proof: test-limit.spec.ts (RED: prod returns null).
- F456 FIXED (pre-landed, verified) — coverageGap has a DERIVED clearing seam (clearCoverageGapIfRecovered on overlap-confirmed reach-back) + scripts/clear-coverage-gap.ts fallback.
- F457 FIXED — engineless keyword lane returns a distinct `'unwired'` outcome: NOT counted dispatched, lane NOT advanced (stays RED). Proof: collector-pacer.service.spec.ts "F457: an engineless keyword lane is RED".
- F458–F461 STOPPED (verified premise, deferred as a unit) — connectionsCreated confirmed structurally 0 (traced through unified totalConnectionsCreated → subResult.databaseOperations.connectionsCreated → all hardcoded-0 producers at extraction-pipeline/keyword-orchestrator). The correct action is deletion of the four metrics, but it is a cross-cutting plumbing change spanning ~15 sites across 8+ files + their specs (batch-processing-queue.types, unified-processing.types, extraction-pipeline, keyword-search-orchestrator, {archive,chronological,keyword}-batch workers, reddit-batch-processing, unified-processing). Doing it piecemeal risks an inconsistent half-deleted state and the green build in a concurrently-edited tree; it warrants a dedicated focused pass ("delete or correct EACH"). NOT done to avoid destabilization.
- F462 FIXED — declared-vs-actual now records the REAL chokepoint count (`postsResult.performance.apiCallsUsed`) instead of the modeled `ceil(outputDocs/100)` that re-derived the estimate's own arithmetic. Threaded `listingRequests` into recordSourceFacts at both call sites.
- F463 FIXED — archive-processing-metrics.service.ts (333-line fabricated-thresholds factory) deleted; injection + recordFileMetrics + recordProcessingMetrics + module provider removed; only stale dist artifacts remained on re-grep.
- F464 FIXED — isNonRetryableError uses typed exceptions (DataConversionException, and Prisma known-request/validation error codes via the unwrapped `cause`), not substring matching.
- F465 FIXED (pre-landed, verified) — dead in-memory retryCount mutation + false 'scheduling retry' log removed; Bull's attemptsMade is the honest count.

Every refusal renders ONE indistinguishable unavailable card, so it never leaks WHICH rule refused. `blockedByAuthorGate` forces an EXPLICIT per-kind decision (identity-adjacent passes an author id, identity-free passes null) so adding a SharedEntityKind cannot silently omit the check — the type-list disease, cured structurally. CROSS-CUTTING (this territory): (1) FOUR surfaces independently answer "is this a saveable/viewable restaurant" — addItem, createUploadTicket, recordRestaurantView, share-package-resolver — with four different subsets of {redirect-resolve, type, status}; only the resolver checks status, only it is right. ONE resolver is the rederivation (F602/F621/F661/F682). (2) The archived-leak class is REAL here but confined to the HOME reads (F692) and latent elsewhere — the user anchors themselves are clean, and that is rehomeEntityAnchors' doing, verified against the mirror. (3) Two audits in this repo check PRESENCE where TRUTH is the property that matters: the signal audit (F690 — a false reason passes) and the env-flag lockdown (F693 — a territory-scoped guard for a repo-wide law).

### the numbers onboarding shows a user

F800 | mobile-app-core | src/screens/Onboarding.tsx:688-693,1698,1704,1743-1745,1593 | **THE NO-FAKE-ESTIMATES LAW IS VIOLATED ON THE APP'S FIRST SCREENS, IN DOLLARS.** `baselineRegretRate = 0.35` and `craveRegretRate = 0.08` are invented priors. They are multiplied by a spend figure derived from two more invented tables (`FREQUENCY_TO_MONTHLY` :91, `BUDGET_TO_AMOUNT` :98) and rendered to the user as money: "you'll redirect ~$X/month toward meals actually worth your time" (:1704), "$Y/month saved from disappointing meals" (:1744, a bare `* 0.33`), "4x less time and effort" (:1743), "11 in 12 meals hit" (:1698). The calendar visualization is seeded at `generateCalendar(0.37)` with a comment that admits the tuning: `// Visual: 35-40% disappoint (copy says "1 in 3")` (:1593) — the number was chosen to match the copy, which is the definition of a seeded prior. The bar charts are hardcoded widths (100%/25%, 100%/50%/11%) presented as measurement. LADDER — compensates-for: the onboarding needs a payoff beat before any data about THIS user exists. bedrock: we have no regret-rate measurement and cannot have one pre-install. rederive: the OnboardingTeaser step (:src/screens/onboarding/OnboardingTeaser.tsx) is the correct shape and already exists — it shows REAL ranked dishes with REAL Crave scores from the teaser endpoint, and degrades to a qualitative fallback when the server has nothing. The regret/time-saved/calendar graph steps should say the same thing QUALITATIVELY (no currency, no rates, no fabricated bar ratios) or be replaced by the teaser. red-team: "it's marketing, everyone does it" — these are specific per-user dollar claims computed from the user's own answers, which reads as a calculation, not a slogan; it is also an App Review 2.3.1/3.1 exposure and it is the exact class the owner's law forbids. | delete every fabricated rate/ratio and the currency copy; keep the teaser as the payoff beat | OWNER-DECISION (2026-08-03 — KEPT AS A CONVERSION EXCEPTION). The owner ruled the animated economics screen stays EXACTLY as shipped, invented numbers and all: it is the app's only animated screen and is deliberately retained as a conversion aid, possibly removed later. This is an explicit, named exception to the no-fake-estimates law, not a disagreement with the finding — the evidence above stands. A Phase-3 deletion had been implemented (three dead graph cases, the two invented lookup tables, the currency formatter, the regret-rate machinery; ~350 lines) and was REVERTED byte-for-byte on the ruling; Onboarding.tsx now carries only the F810 change. THE EXCEPTION MUST BE RE-CONFIRMED IF THE SCREEN IS EVER REDESIGNED — a redesign is a new decision, and the numbers do not inherit the exemption. F801 (the fabricated 25/12 defaults) rides the same exception | read the three graph cases + their consumers

### the api seam

F803 | mobile-app-core | eas.json:10 vs src/services/api.ts:11,242 | **EVERY EAS BUILD POINTS AT THE WRONG BASE URL.** `build.base.env.EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL = "https://api-production-a56f.up.railway.app"` — no `/api/v1`. The api client treats that value as the COMPLETE base (its own default is `http://localhost:3000/api/v1`, and the health probe strips `/api/v1` off the end to find `/health`), and the NestJS app sets `setGlobalPrefix('api/v1')` (apps/api/src/main.ts:181). So a development/preview/production EAS build calls `https://…/search`, `https://…/users/me` and 404s on everything. It has not bitten because the day-to-day sim reads `.env.local`, which carries the suffix. The base-URL truth lives in four places (eas.json, .env.example, .env.local, the DEFAULT_API_URL constant) and three of them agree. | one place: put the `/api/v1` suffix INSIDE the client (`baseURL = ${origin}/api/v1`) and let the env carry only an origin; then a missing suffix is impossible | FIXED (D38) — eas.json's `build.base.env.EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL` now carries the `/api/v1` global prefix, so every EAS profile (development/development-device/preview/production) calls a real base instead of 404ing on the origin. The agreement between the four homes of that URL is now ASSERTED, not trusted: services/api-base-url.spec.ts checks every EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL in eas.json, the .env.example template, and the client's DEFAULT_API_URL fallback. RED-PROVED: restoring the suffix-less value fails 'every EAS build profile points at a complete base URL' | read main.ts prefix + all four env homes

F804 | mobile-app-core | src/services/api.ts:304-383 | **THE ONE CLIENT HAS NO 401 SEAM.** The response interceptor handles 403+ENTITLEMENT_REQUIRED and >=500, and nothing else. A 401 (expired/revoked Clerk session, or `getAuthToken` returning null because the token resolver threw — :265-271 swallows that into `logger.warn` + null) falls through to the generic failure modal, and the app keeps making unauthenticated requests forever with no sign-out and no re-auth prompt. The AuthProvider's resolver is the only refresh path and it has no retry. LADDER — compensates-for: nothing; the case was simply never written. bedrock: Clerk owns refresh, but the CLIENT owns "the refresh failed, now what". rederive: one `on401` branch beside the 403 branch — clear the query cache, announce through a session-lapse store the way `entitlementLapseStore` does for entitlement, and let the route coordinator show sign-in. red-team: "Clerk auto-refreshes so 401 can't happen" — it can (revoked session, deleted user, clock skew, the resolver's own catch), and today the failure mode is an app that silently degrades to anonymous. | FIXED (D38) — ONE explicit 401 seam. `resolveApiFailureAction` (services/api-failure-policy.ts) closes the outcome set — session_lapse / entitlement_lapse / service_issue / caller_owned — and the response interceptor now dispatches on it instead of testing two ad-hoc conditions and letting 401 fall through. A 401 announces through `useSessionLapseStore` (mirroring entitlementLapseStore), tags the error `isSessionLapse`, and AppRouteCoordinator makes the SERVER's verdict win over Clerk's cached `isSignedIn`: authStatus becomes 'signed_out', the react-query cache is cleared, and the route is sign-in. Silent-anonymous is no longer representable. The lapse clears on a real session change (signed out, or a new user id). MUTATION-PROVED: api-failure-policy.spec.ts, 8 pass, incl. 'THE TRANSITION: a 401 flips the app into an explicit signed-out state'; deleting the `status === 401` case fails 2 cases including the store transition | read the whole interceptor + AuthProvider token resolver

Nothing types the call site into remembering them, and the consequence of forgetting is invisible in code review — a background poll that 500s raises the app-wide "Service temporarily unavailable" banner. `usersService.getMe` remembers; most callers don't. | make it explicit at the seam: `api.get(url, quiet())` or a second exported client (`backgroundApi`) whose interceptor never reports | PROPOSED | grep suppressSystemStatus across src

F807 | mobile-app-core | metro.config.js:24-41 | **DEAD CONFIG POINTING AT FILES THAT DO NOT EXIST.** `config.resolver.alias = { 'react-native$': …/src/shims/reactNativeProxy.js, 'react-native/Libraries/Utilities/codegenNativeComponent': …/src/shims/codegenNativeComponentShim.js, '@rnmapbox/maps': …, 'react-native-svg': … }`. ### errors that reach nobody

F810 | mobile-app-core | src/screens/Onboarding.tsx:451-463 | **SILENT LOSS OF THE ONE THING ONBOARDING EXISTS TO COLLECT.** When the user is signed in and `usersService.completeOnboarding` throws, the catch logs a warn, CLEARS the service-issue banner (`clearServiceIssue('global')` — actively hiding the evidence), and marks onboarding complete LOCALLY. The persisted status is now `completed`, so the flow never runs again and the payload — every answer, plus the username claim — is never retried or replayed. The user believes they finished; the server has nothing. There is no outbox and no reconciliation on next launch. LADDER — compensates-for: a real fear, that a network blip strands a user on the last onboarding screen. bedrock: the answers are the product's cold-start signal; losing them silently is worse than a retry. rederive: complete locally AND enqueue the payload for replay (the answers already live in the persisted zustand draft — keep the draft until the server confirms, and re-send on next authenticated launch). What becomes impossible: a completed-locally user whose answers never landed. | keep the draft until server-confirmed; replay on launch | FIXED (D38) — the answers are DURABLE DATA (owner, 2026-08-03) and can no longer be discarded. The catch no longer clears the service-issue banner and no longer marks onboarding complete: it records the exact rejected payload in a PERSISTED outbox (`onboardingStore.pendingServerCompletion`, in `partialize` so it survives a cold start), surfaces the failure through `completionError` (the CTA stays live, so pressing it again retries), and returns false. AppRouteCoordinator replays the payload on the next authenticated launch via `decideOnboardingCompletionReplay`; only a CONFIRMED server write empties the outbox, and it NEVER expires — a payload we cannot land today is still the only copy of the user's answers. RED-PROVED: onboarding-completion-replay.spec.ts drives the REAL persisted store through a REAL failing call, 8 pass; restoring the old catch (completeOnboardingLocally + return true) fails 4, including 'RED — a failing server call does NOT mark onboarding complete' and 'the payload survives VERBATIM, username claim included' | read completeAndEnterApp + onboardingStore.completeOnboardingLocally

They tap the shutter and the app does nothing, twice, three times. The app has a standard failure surface (`announceFailureIfOnline`) that this bypasses. | announce, or set an inline error state | PROPOSED | read the handler + the modal store's API

F812 | mobile-app-core | src/screens/EntitlementLapseHost.tsx:23-25 | `void access.refresh().then(…)` with NO `.catch`. The probe fires precisely when the server just returned 403 — the likeliest next outcome is that `/users/me` fails too, producing an unhandled promise rejection inside the paywall takeover. | `.catch(() => {})` is not the fix; the honest one is to leave the wall up and log through the crash-reporting seam | PROPOSED | read the effect

The seam exists and is used in exactly one place. | route these through `captureHandledError` with a `seam:` tag, the way the mutation chokepoint does | PROPOSED | read crash-reporting's exports + every warn site in the auth path

### argued ideal

F819 | mobile-app-core | src/services/purchases.ts (whole file) | **IDEAL, argued.** Build it again today and this is the shape. Every identity mutation chains through ONE promise queue (`enqueueIdentity`), so an interleaved logOut/logIn cannot leave RevenueCat pointing at the wrong user while the app believes otherwise; `purchasePackage` REFUSES unless RC's configured identity equals the caller's signed-in Clerk id, which closes the "user charged, purchase unmappable" hole at its only door; access truth is stated to live in the server ledger and the file refuses to gate on `CustomerInfo`; and a release build carrying a `test_` key disables purchases loudly rather than shipping the Test Store to App Review. The one thing each guard protects is nameable, and each can show RED. | — | IDEAL-VERIFIED | read alongside PurchasesProvider + useAccess

F820 | mobile-app-core | src/store/systemStatusStore.ts + src/providers/NetworkStatusListener.tsx | **IDEAL, argued.** ONE online truth with a single writer: NetInfo writes the store, the store MIRRORS into react-query's `onlineManager` (and is the only writer of it), so banner, search pause/resume, react-query refetch and the dev failure-matrix lever cannot disagree. `subscribeToReconnect` makes the offline→online EDGE a shared primitive instead of five hand-rolled detectors. The `devOfflineOverride` is `__DEV__`-fenced in the setter itself, not at the call site. The foreground re-`fetch` is justified by a named NetInfo failure mode. | — | IDEAL-VERIFIED | read both plus every consumer of isOffline

F821 | mobile-app-core | src/services/device-key.ts | **IDEAL, argued.** A best-effort keychain install id with an explicit "NEVER throws, never blocks a request" contract that the code actually holds; no `Math.random` fallback, because a fabricated weak id would poison the clustering signal it exists to feed (the no-fake-values law, applied one level below where anyone would think to apply it); an in-flight promise so concurrent requests mint once; and a comment explaining why `expo-crypto` is deliberately NOT require()'d (Metro resolves require statically, so a guarded require of an absent module still breaks the bundle). Even the decision to cache a null answer is argued rather than assumed. | — | IDEAL-VERIFIED | read with the api request interceptor

F822 | mobile-app-core | src/store/viewport-subject-store.ts:148-166 | **IDEAL, argued** (and a pattern worth copying): `useViewportSubjectVerdict` exists beside `useViewportSubjectState` because subscribing to the whole state re-rendered every place-name mouth on each settle. The narrow hook is only correct because `verdict` is carried BY REFERENCE through the partial-merge write, and the comment says exactly that — the invariant that makes the optimization safe is written down next to it. | — | IDEAL-VERIFIED | read the store + the merge writer

### the services layer (F830–F849)

EXECUTED: `npx jest --silent` from apps/mobile → **51 suites / 480 tests pass, exit 0** — and a worker DIED mid-run (F830).
Every `api.*` path in search/polls/photos/user-lists/messaging was cross-checked against the NestJS controllers:
**all match** (search.controller 7, history 4, polls 15, photos 10, user-lists 21 + public + share, messaging 10). No 404s.
No spec anywhere exercises `services/{search,polls,photos,user-lists,messaging}.ts` or `useSearchRequests.ts`.

F830 | mobile-app-core | src/overlays/panels/save-list-model.spec.ts:12-18 + src/store/saved-membership-store.ts:82,93 | **THE SUITE PRINTS GREEN WHILE A WORKER DIES.** The spec partially mocks `services/user-lists` with three methods; the membership store's 50ms `setTimeout` flush calls `batchMemberships`, which the mock lacks, so the timer fires after teardown and Node prints `TypeError: userListsService.batchMemberships is not a function` — and jest still reports `51 passed`, exit 0. This is CLAUDE.md's own always-green disease inside the instrument that is supposed to catch it: a genuine unhandled rejection is invisible to CI. | add `batchMemberships` to the mock; export `resetSavedMembershipQueue()` and call it in `afterEach`; make an unhandled rejection fail the run | FIXED (D38) — the always-green disease is out of the instrument itself, in three parts. (1) THE LEAK: `save-list-model.spec.ts` mocks `batchMemberships` (the method its import graph reaches, not just the ones it calls) and calls the new `resetSavedMembershipQueue()` in afterEach, which cancels the 50ms flush timer. (2) THE OTHER LEAK, found while fixing this one and NOT in the finding: the transition engine's join-liveness watchdog was never disarmed when a txn left 'joining', so every transition left a live 600ms timer — that was what force-killed the worker in the full run. `advance`/`supersede` now disarm it, `resetTransitionTxnHolderForTest` disarms every armed watchdog (a txn a spec created directly was never in the holder), and the spec calls it in afterEach. (3) THE GATE: `yarn test` now runs `scripts/jest-honest.js`, which streams jest's output and FAILS the run on 'A worker process has failed to exit gracefully', 'A worker process has crashed', 'Cannot log after tests are done', and the force-exit notices. RED-PROVED: with the watchdog leak present, jest exits 0 while the runner exits 1 naming the marker (captured run: 53 suites / 486 tests 'passed', 12 post-teardown logs, jest exit 0, runner exit 1). SURPRISE WORTH RECORDING: in-band (single-file) the crash DOES exit 1, but with 2+ suites the worker death is swallowed ENTIRELY — no message at all, exit 0. The always-green window is wider than the finding described. NOW: 58 suites / 526 tests, exit 0, no markers | reproduced in the jest run

A timer-issued request has no user waiting on it and must never speak for the whole app; a user-initiated request must. rederive: invert the axis — `api.ts` exports `USER_INITIATED` / `SILENT` and every call site picks one, or a `pollingRequest()` wrapper sets it. | invert the default; one exported pair | PROPOSED | read the panels' refetchIntervals + the interceptor
