F850 | mobile-app-core | src/perf/ui-frame-sampler.ts:60-79 + PerfScenarioCoordinator.tsx:231-236,946-954 | **A MISSING NATIVE SAMPLER IS INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM A PERFECT 60fps RUN.** `startUiFrameSampler` returns a no-op stop when the module is absent; the only notice is triple-gated `__DEV__ && EXPO_PUBLIC_PERF_UI_SAMPLER_MISSING_LOG==='1' && !hasLoggedOnce` — so on the RELEASE lane, the exact lane the coordinator's own comment says "the 60fps assertion must be measurable on", that log is unreachable BY CONSTRUCTION. Name the defect that makes this instrument show RED: there isn't one. A missing module, a renamed `UI_FRAME_SAMPLER_MODULE_NAME`, a native `start` that no-ops — all produce zero output, which reads as zero bad windows. Compounding it, `scenario_sampling_started` logs `activeConfig.uiFrameSampler.enabled` — a URL PARAMETER, i.e. intent — while the failed-attach no-op is pushed into `stopCallbacks` indistinguishably. | emit `ui_sampler_attached: true|false, reason` unconditionally on the window channel; carry `uiSamplerAttached` in the scenario summary; log the START RETURN, never the config | FIXED (D38) — `startUiFrameSampler` returns an ATTACHMENT (`{attached, reason, detail, platform, stop}`) instead of a bare stop function, so the caller is handed the verdict and cannot ignore it (the type change broke the coordinator at compile time, which is the point). Three named failure reasons: native_module_missing, native_module_malformed, native_start_threw (the native `start` is now wrapped). The coordinator attaches BEFORE logging `scenario_sampling_started` and reports the START RETURN — `uiFrameSamplerAttached` + `uiFrameSamplerAttachFailure` — beside the renamed intent fields (`*Requested`); it also emits an `attach` event on the UiFrameSampler channel itself. The triple-gated **DEV**/env/once log, unreachable on the Release lane by construction, is DELETED. MUTATION-PROVED: perf-sampler-honesty.spec.ts drives a missing module, a malformed module and a throwing start; making the missing path claim `attached:true` fails 3 cases | read both samplers + the coordinator's start path

F851 | mobile-app-core | src/perf/js-frame-sampler.ts:158-162 + js-task-latency-sampler.ts:132,150-152 | **THE WORST STALL IS THE ONE BOTH SAMPLERS DISCARD.** A frame gap above `MAX_TRACKED_FRAME_MS` (5000) is DROPPED and the window flushed; a lag above `MAX_TRACKED_LAG_MS` falls to `flushWindow` without incrementing `stallCount`. F880 | mobile-app-core | src/components/app-modal-store.ts, share-modal-store.ts, option-selector-store.ts, score-info-store.ts, collaborator-modal-store.ts, list-edit-store.ts + their six hosts | **THE BIGGEST STRUCTURAL ITEM IN THIS TERRITORY, AND IT HAS ALREADY COST THREE BUGS.** Six hand-rolled copies of the same imperative singleton store (`let current = null`, `const listeners = new Set()`, `emit`, `show/close`, `getSnapshot`, `subscribe` — ~35 lines apiece), plus six copy-pasted `useSurfaceHost` ref dances in the hosts (AppModalHost:31-35, ShareModalHost:370-374, OptionSelectorHost:20-24, ScoreInfoHost:20-24, CollaboratorModalHost:239-243, ListEditHost:172-176). They have DRIFTED in exactly the way duplicated primitives do: `dismissAppModal`/`dismissShareModal` take an optional IDENTITY argument to defeat the close-races-a-newer-open bug (app-modal-store.ts:78-87, the fix documented at AppModalHost.tsx:67-70) — `closeScoreInfo`, `closeCollaboratorModal` and `closeListEdit` DO NOT, so each still carries the bug that was found and fixed twice; `closeOptionSelector` invented a third, key-matched variant. LADDER — compensates-for: nothing; the second store copied the first before the identity fix existed. rederive: one `createSingletonSurfaceStore<T>()` returning `{show, close(identity?), getSnapshot, subscribe, useValue}` with the identity-scoped close BUILT IN, plus a `useSurfaceHost(store)` hook owning the last-payload-through-exit-animation. What becomes impossible: a surface that forgets the race fix. ~200 lines deleted, three latent races closed. red-team: "score-info is scene-scoped in search, so it's genuinely different" — that is a second INSTANCE of the store, not a second KIND; the factory serves it. | one factory + one host hook | FIXED (D38) — ONE `createSingletonSurfaceStore<TPayload, TIdentity>()` (components/singleton-surface-store.ts) with the identity-scoped close BUILT IN, plus `useSingletonSurfaceHost(store)` owning the last-payload-through-exit-animation and a close scoped to the payload the render drew. All six stores are instances (app-modal, share-modal, option-selector, score-info, collaborator-modal, list-edit) and all six hosts use the hook; ~200 lines of copied `let current / listeners / emit / show / close / getSnapshot / subscribe` and six copy-pasted ref dances are gone. The three stores that never got the race fix (closeScoreInfo, closeCollaboratorModal, closeListEdit) now have it, and the option selector's third, key-matched variant is expressed as the factory's `identityOf` parameter rather than a second implementation. A surface that forgets the race fix is unrepresentable. SURPRISE: making `closeListEdit` take an identity immediately caught a live bug the types had been hiding — ListEditHost passed it straight to `onPress`, so a GestureResponderEvent was arriving where the identity goes; under the new close that would silently refuse to close, and it is now `() => closeListEdit(payload)`. RED-PROVED: singleton-surface-store.spec.ts, 8 pass; deleting the identity guard fails 'THE RACE: a deferred close cannot kill the surface that replaced it' and the custom-identity case | diffed all six stores and all six hosts

<!-- D37 Phase-3 implementation, 2026-08-03. Owner-ruled restoration (business/business-model.md "Margin lever"). NOTE: F757 was claimed by the api-search work above before this entry landed; this is F759. -->

F759 | api-identity-money | apps/api/src/modules/billing/billing.service.ts, billing.controller.ts, dto/create-checkout-session.dto.ts, prisma/schema.prisma (CheckoutSession + checkout_session_status), migrations/20260803130000_restore_stripe_checkout_rail, config/configuration.ts (stripe.\*) | THE STRIPE WEB CHECKOUT RAIL WAS MISSING FOR THE PLAN THAT NOW DEPENDS ON IT. Deleted 2026-07-09 in c2861853 under the then-true rationale "models a rail that doesn't exist"; owner ruled 2026-08-01/08-03 that web checkout IS the plan (Strava dual-button paywall, ~97% net vs ~85% via StoreKit, Apple-commission-free out-of-app under the April 2025 Epic contempt ruling). Without it the primary paywall button had no URL to open. | RESTORED where the old shape was still right (hosted Checkout session creation, the attempt-log table, the portal session, `checkout.session.completed`). RE-DERIVED onto today's tree where it was not: (1) the DTO no longer accepts client `successUrl`/`cancelUrl` — an open redirect wearing a feature's clothes; all three redirect URLs plus the price id come from config with NO defaults (no-fake-estimates: a guessed price sells the wrong thing, a guessed redirect strands a payer), and an unconfigured rail 503s naming the missing env vars. (2) SINGLE-PRODUCT LAW is now enforced on BOTH doors: a `priceId` that is not the configured premium price is a 400 naming both ids, and a subscription arriving with an `entitlement_code` outside the closed ledger vocabulary throws (event recorded FAILED + replayable) instead of minting a paid grant under a code `accessVerdict()` never asks about — the same D1-residual hole `RevenueCatEntitlementMap` refuses at boot, closed on the Stripe side. (3) ENTITLEMENTS UNIFY THROUGH THE ACCESS-GRANT LEDGER, not the restored table: `checkout.session.completed` retrieves the subscription and funnels into the same `syncSubscriptionGrant` the subscription events use, idempotent per `stripe:<subId>`, so whichever of the two events Stripe delivers first is sufficient and the second is a no-op. The table is the ATTEMPT log only. (4) `subscription_data.metadata` is stamped at session creation — Stripe does NOT copy Checkout Session metadata onto the subscription, so without it every web subscription event would log "without matching user" and grant nothing. (5) `ensureStripeCustomer` came back into BillingService rather than UserService: it is billing's business and nothing else ever called it. (6) The recreated table is timestamptz throughout and FK RESTRICT, per the laws that postdate its deletion. | FIXED (2026-08-03) | PROOF: `npx jest billing entitlements` 86/86 green (12 new in billing-checkout.spec.ts, stripe SDK mocked — zero live vendor calls); `yarn build` clean; migration applied locally and `prisma migrate diff` shows ZERO checkout drift. **AMENDED 2026-08-03 (same day, owner ruling): TWO PRICES OF THE ONE PRODUCT, and the single-product law got STRONGER, not looser.** The owner ruled that the web paywall shows the SAME structure as the in-app one (Strava pattern: same offers, different payment location) — $7.99/mo and $39.99/yr with an annual-only ~1-week free trial. So `stripe.premiumPriceId` (STRIPE_PREMIUM_PRICE_ID) became the closed pair `stripe.prices.{monthly,annual}` (STRIPE_MONTHLY_PRICE_ID / STRIPE_ANNUAL_PRICE_ID), and the DTO's `priceId` field was DELETED in favour of `{ plan: 'monthly' | 'annual' }`. Note what this does to the law: the original shape enforced 'premium is the only product' by comparing a client-supplied opaque Stripe id against the configured one — a refusal. A two-word vocabulary makes a price id outside the configured pair UNREPRESENTABLE, because the caller cannot name a price at all; the api alone maps word -> price. One PRODUCT, two PRICES of it; `entitlement_code` metadata is identical on both plans, so both grant the same thing `accessVerdict()` asks about. `plan` is REQUIRED, never defaulted — a paywall showing two prices against a body that silently means 'monthly' is a charge the user did not pick. The trial lives in the SESSION (`subscription_data.trial_period_days = ANNUAL_TRIAL_PERIOD_DAYS = 7`, annual only, spread in rather than set-to-undefined) not on the Stripe price, so the offer the copy promises and the offer the code builds are the same object. `requireCheckoutConfig()` requires BOTH prices regardless of the requested plan: a half-configured rail must 503 on the FIRST request, not on the unlucky user who picks the other button. PROOF: `npx jest billing --silent` 53/53 green; `yarn build` clean; zero live vendor calls (stripe SDK still mocked wholesale). MUTATION-PROVEN RED, three recipes: (a) `dto.plan === 'annual'` -> `'monthly'` (trial on the wrong plan) = 2 red; (b) `config.prices[dto.plan]` -> `config.prices.monthly` (plan ignored) = 1 red; (c) reopening the DTO to `@IsOptional() @IsString() plan` = 4 red; all restored, 53/53 green. ORIGINAL-PASS PROOF: replacing the single-product price check with `if (false)` and the replayed-event short-circuit with `if (false)` turns exactly 2 tests red ("REFUSES a second product", "idempotent on REPLAY — zero extra grants"); restored, 12/12 green. NOT DONE (out of scope, reported): no Stripe key was used, nothing deployed, mobile client not wired.

F706/F707 RESOLUTION (2026-08-03) | OWNER-RULED: RAIL RETIRED. "The map is in a good state, that's locked in, and we no longer need to debug it the way we did when we created that rail." Deleted: visual-baselines/ (4 PNGs + README), scripts/visual-regression.js + .test.js, both package.json entries, the stale @run-by cross-reference. perf-scenario-visual-contracts.js is NOT part of the rail (perf-verb contracts, owner-KEEP seed) and survives. The eye is the map's regression oracle now, by explicit owner choice. | OWNER-DECISION → DELETED

<!-- D39 site rederivation, 2026-08-03. Owner-ruled: the public website must carry web payments end-to-end. -->

F760 | site + api-identity-money | apps/site/** (new workspace: src/config.ts, src/clerk-frontend-api.ts, src/premium-page.ts, src/router.ts, src/server.ts, src/pages/{index,privacy,terms,support}.html, test/router.test.ts, Dockerfile, README.md, package.json, tsconfig.json), railway.site.json, apps/api/src/main.ts (CORS) | THE PUBLIC WEBSITE HAD NO SOURCE. craveapp.ai was a Railway bun "function" whose ENTIRE source was a base64 blob inside the service's `startCommand` — four HTML constants and an if-chain, outside the repo, outside git, outside review, un-diffable and un-testable. D39 ruled it must carry web payments (Strava dual-button, ~97% net); that shape cannot hold auth or checkout at all. SECOND, INDEPENDENT DEFECT FOUND WHILE WIRING IT: prod CORS was a flat `origin: false`. Correct while the only client was the native app (not a browser, sends no Origin) — but it makes the browser's POST /billing/checkout-session unreachable, so F759's restored rail had a URL nobody could ask for. The web rail was DEAD ON ARRIVAL in prod and nothing said so. | REDERIVED as apps/site, a zero-dependency node:http server (runs unchanged on Bun; node:http chosen over Bun.serve so the shipped server is the one the tests exercise — bun is not installed on the dev machine, and a test that cannot run is not a test). (1) The four pages are PRESERVED, captured from the live site, with exactly one edit: Cloudflare had rewritten every support@craveapp.ai link into `__cf_email__` obfuscation that renders only if a CDN script loads — those are plain mailto: links again, and a test asserts no cdn-cgi/**cf_email** residue survives. (2) /premium: Clerk sign-in for the SAME instance as the app, then a JWT-TEMPLATE bearer to `${API_ORIGIN}/api/v1/billing/checkout-session` with an EMPTY body, then follow the returned hosted-Stripe url. Empty body is the point: the site names no price (single-product law is the api's to enforce) and no redirect URL (the DTO stopped accepting client successUrl/cancelUrl — an open redirect wearing a feature's clothes); /premium/success and /premium/cancelled exist to BE the configured STRIPE*CHECKOUT*\*\_URL targets, not to be passed as them. Tests assert /priceId/ and /successUrl|cancelUrl/ NEVER appear in the served page. (3) The Clerk frontend-API host is DERIVED from the publishable key (which is base64 of that host) rather than being a fourth env var — a hand-typed host that disagrees with the key signs visitors into the wrong Clerk instance and the api then rejects every token; a malformed key throws naming the reason instead of guessing, including a hostname-shape check so a corrupt key cannot inject a path into the script src. (4) NO SECRET LIVES HERE: no Stripe key, no Clerk secret key, no DB URL. Env contract is three non-secret vars (API_ORIGIN, CLERK_PUBLISHABLE_KEY, PORT=8080) plus CLERK_JWT_TEMPLATE. (5) An unconfigured checkout is an OUTAGE and must look like one: /premium answers 503 naming the missing vars, while the four static pages keep serving — a billing misconfiguration must never take the Privacy Policy offline. (6) api CORS now reads WEB_ORIGIN (comma-separated exact origins) and, UNSET, leaves prod EXACTLY as strict as before — an allowlist, not a loosening, and a config-less deploy changes nothing. credentials stays false (bearer header, never a cookie). | FIXED (2026-08-03) | PROOF: 12/12 `node --test` green in apps/site; root `yarn build` green (4/4 turbo tasks, site included). MUTATION-PROVEN RED: changing /premium's unconfigured status 503->200 and dropping the trailing-slash normalization turns exactly 2 tests red ("/premium is 503 (not a broken 200) when unconfigured", "trailing slashes and case do not 302 a real page away"); restored, 12/12 green. ZERO VENDOR CALLS in tests (the fixture publishable key is base64 of "fake-clerk.example.com$"). NOT DONE (out of scope, reported): nothing staged, committed, or deployed; Railway untouched; the base64-startCommand function is still the live site until the owner executes the cutover in apps/site/README.md. **AMENDED 2026-08-03 (owner ruling, paired with F759's amendment): /premium NOW SHOWS BOTH OFFERS.\*\* The page shipped with a single 'Continue to secure checkout' button posting an empty body — which, once the api grew two prices, would have quietly steered every web buyer onto whichever price the api happened to pick as the default. The Strava pattern is the SAME offers in a different payment location, not a reduced menu, so /premium renders two plan cards in the existing dark/light visual language: $7.99/mo (Cancel anytime) and $39.99/yr (1 week free, then $39.99/year), each button posting `{"plan":"…"}` built from its own `data-plan`. The 'never names a price' property SURVIVES and is now structural: the page cannot express a Stripe price id, only a plan word. HONEST COPY IS TESTED, not just present — the annual note must say what happens AFTER the trial ('1 week free, then $39.99/year'); '1 week free' alone is the half-truth that produces chargebacks — and a test asserts the trial is never promised on monthly. The dollar amounts and the trial length are COPY, not configuration: they must match the Stripe prices and ANNUAL_TRIAL_PERIOD_DAYS, and the README says so, because only the api can charge — a mismatch is a LIE ON THE PAGE, not a wrong charge, which is worse rather than better. Both buttons disable together while either is in flight (one impatient double-click must not open two payment pages for one decision). Still zero dependencies, zero secrets, zero vendor calls. PROOF: `yarn workspace site build` + `test` 13/13 green. MUTATION-PROVEN RED: (d) hard-coding the POST body to `{"plan":"monthly"}` (ignoring the button) = 1 red; (e) deleting the annual offer card = 2 red; restored, 13/13 green. OPEN DEPENDENCY: CLERK_JWT_TEMPLATE defaults to 'mobile' because that is the ONLY template known to mint an audience CLERK_JWT_AUDIENCE accepts — a dedicated 'web' template requires adding its aud to the api first, or every checkout 401s.

Two laws it encodes: unknown KEYS are preserved verbatim on write and merely reported (`unknownQuestionIds`) — the document is the user's testimony and the decoder has opinions at READ only; unknown OPTION ids are dropped at read and COUNTED (`droppedOptionIds`), so a drop can be seen. (3) `source` ('completion' | 'replay' | 'edit' | 'backfill') keeps "answered at the end of the flow" distinguishable from "landed later from the outbox" forever. (4) The DTO validates THROUGH the decoder — proving the document is DECODABLE, not filtering it (rejecting unknown keys would make every question-set rollout a coordinated deploy). (5) REPLACE-WHOLE-DOCUMENT is stated as law, with the partial-edit endpoint named as a future separate door — never a PATCH wearing a PUT's clothes. | FIXED (2026-08-03) | PROOF: 5 cases in apps/api/src/modules/identity/onboarding-completion-write.spec.ts asserting both writes land INSIDE the one $transaction; migration applied locally — 2 users with answers -> 2 history rows, 2 distinct users. The code checks ONLY `phase !== 'staged' && phase !== 'committed'` — it never checks that the pending set is full. Line 416 then does `live.pendingJoinInputs = new Set(nextInputs)` UNCONDITIONALLY, so an already-landed input is RESURRECTED into pending. Failure scenario: BottomSheetSceneStackHost.tsx:1491 amends on a genuine roles change; if a `paint` offer already landed in the 'committed' window (e.g. the warm evidence path at :1499 on a prior frame), the amend un-lands it and the join now waits for a paint that will never re-fire on a warm leg -> 600ms watchdog degrade every switch. Ladder: compensated-for = the host being the only place that knows cold-vs-warm; bedrock = the host declares the join, once, at arm; rederive = make the amendment a first-class 'arm' edge (staged|committed -> armed) that is legal exactly once and preserves already-landed marks, instead of a mutable rewrite of readonly-ish plan data via a cast. | ESCALATED (design) | read; cast at :415 `(live.plan as {joinInputs: ...}).joinInputs = nextInputs` mutates a field the type declares readonly

F902 | mobile-nav-overlays | apps/mobile/src/navigation/runtime/transition-engine/transition-transaction.ts:255-258 vs apps/mobile/src/overlays/BottomSheetSceneStackHost.tsx:1486-1500 | CALLER-MUST-REMEMBER + SILENT NO-OP. `sealTransitionTxnJoin` returns silently when `phase !== 'committed'`. The host's arm site explicitly admits phase may be 'staged' (`liveTxnForArm?.phase === 'staged' || === 'committed'`) and then calls amend + `sealLiveTransitionTxnJoin()`. In the 'staged' case the seal is a NO-OP, and the warm-evidence `offerTransitionJoinInput('paint')` two lines later is ALSO a no-op (offer requires phase joining|committed, :429-435). Both the seal and the warm paint evidence are silently dropped; the txn later seals at idle with a pending 'paint' that a warm leg never re-fires -> guaranteed 600ms watchdog degrade. Neither drop barks. Ladder: bedrock = an arm either takes effect or is loud; rederive = seal should either be legal from 'staged' (staged -> joining is a sensible edge) or report a violation, and offer should bark when it drops an input the live plan declared. | ESCALATED (design) | read both sites; LEGAL_EDGES at :112-119 has no staged->joining edge

F903 | mobile-nav-overlays | apps/mobile/src/navigation/runtime/transition-engine/transition-transaction.ts:238-240 + 298, 388, 418, 438, 447 | TRUTH IN 2 PLACES (notification). `advance()` already notifies every listener when the txn is live (:238-240). Five call sites ALSO call `listeners.forEach(...)` immediately after an advance-bearing call: the watchdog (:298), `withLiveTransitionTxn` (:388), `amendTransitionTxnJoinInputs` (:418), `offerTransitionJoinInput` (:438), `sealLiveTransitionTxnJoin` (:447). Every phase-changing path notifies TWICE. Not a correctness bug today (listeners are idempotent readers), but it is exactly the "who notifies" ambiguity that makes a future non-idempotent subscriber (the scene-stack runtime's deferred-flush subscriber at app-route-scene-stack-runtime.ts:2088) fire twice. Ladder: bedrock = one notifier; rederive = `advance()` and `stage()` are the ONLY notifiers; delete the five trailing forEach calls. | ESCALATED (design; trivial but touches the engine everyone is on) | read

F904 | mobile-nav-overlays | apps/mobile/src/navigation/runtime/transition-engine/transition-transaction.ts:341-348 | `supersedeTransitionTxn` deliberately does NOT notify listeners; only its caller `stageTransitionTxn` notifies, AFTER minting the replacement. Consequence: a subscriber waiting for "the txn I armed for has terminated" (app-route-scene-stack-runtime.ts:2088-2105) is woken with a BRAND-NEW live txn in phase 'staged' — none of its terminal conditions match — so it does NOT unsubscribe and does NOT flush. It self-heals on the new txn's revealed edge, but the subscriber is watching WHICHEVER txn is live, not the one it armed for, which is the un-keyed-singleton smell (Q-5) the engine's own header claims to have killed. Ladder: rederive = subscribers register interest in a txnId, and the holder delivers `(txnId, phase)` to the listener rather than making every listener re-read a global. | ESCALATED (design) | read both

The 20-line comment above it explains WHY a watchdog exists and what a fire means, but never what 600 is derived from. It is not a fact, not a stated owner choice, and not a derivation. It is the single number that decides whether a slow real switch is "broken" or "fine" — and per-plan override `joinLivenessMs` exists precisely because 600 was wrong for fade-paced revises ("~2s measured"). F939 | mobile-nav-overlays | apps/mobile/src/overlays/shell-residency-registry.ts:100-120 vs apps/mobile/src/navigation/runtime/ADDING_A_SCENE.md:16-24 | THE ONE REGISTRY THAT ESCAPED THE REPO'S OWN EXHAUSTIVENESS LAW. ADDING_A_SCENE.md states the law plainly — "Let the compiler find the rest. The `Record<OverlayKey>` registries and the completeness assertions turn a forgotten key into a build error that NAMES the key. This exhaustiveness exists specifically because a missing CHILD_SHARED_SHEET_SCENES entry once silently broke nav." The residency registry breaks it three ways: `RESIDENCY_MANAGED_SCENES` is a plain array, not a `Record<OverlayKey>`, so a new scene is silently unmanaged (F908); `residentUnitIdentityOf` ends in `default: return 'scene';`, so a new multi-entry scene silently gets ONE shared singleton resident unit instead of per-identity units — the exact "silently broke nav" class, one layer down; and the doc's section 4 lists the policies that intentionally degrade gracefully, and residency is not among them. Ladder: bedrock = the doc's own law; rederive = make `residentUnitIdentityOf` an exhaustive `Record<ResidencyManagedSceneKey, (entry) => string | null>` so a new managed scene must declare its identity, and derive the key type from the array (F908). | ESCALATED (design) | read both; doc lines 16-24 quoted

Likewise magic numbers: a targeted scan of the four largest controllers (scene-switch, sheet-host-authority, transition-policy-runtime, motion-executor — ~4,800 lines) surfaced exactly ONE unattributed constant (F938); every other literal is a token counter or a stack-length comparison. F941 | mobile-nav-overlays | apps/mobile/src/navigation/runtime/ADDING_A_SCENE.md (192 lines) + apps/mobile/src/overlays/PERFORMANCE_PATTERNS.md (368 lines) | IDEAL-VERIFIED. That last distinction is the thing most such docs get wrong. F942 | mobile-nav-overlays | apps/mobile/src/navigation/RootNavigator.tsx:49-51 | BLOCK THAT CANNOT CHANGE ANYTHING (mutation-tested to a proof, not a hunch). `if (!isReadyToRender) { return routeState.destination === 'main' ? <AppShellMainNavigator /> : null; }`. So for every NON-main destination `isReadyToRender` is necessarily already true at line 49 — the `: null` arm is unreachable. And the `'main'` arm returns `<AppShellMainNavigator />`, which is byte-identical to what `case 'main'` at :76 returns. Delete lines 49-51 entirely: identical behavior on every path. Ladder: compensates-for = an older split where the splash lived above the shell and main had to render early; bedrock = the splash gate now lives INSIDE the main shell (shouldHideSplash, MainLaunchCoordinator.tsx:1032-1044); rederive = drop the block, and `isReadyToRender` stops being consumed by RootNavigator at all — which is the honest statement, since it is a main-launch concept that no longer routes anything. | ESCALATED (deletion of 3 lines, but on the app's front door — wants one boot verification) | read all three files; derivation chain verified line by line

F1000 | mobile-search | runtime/scheduler/runtime-work-scheduler.ts:148-153 + frame-budget-governor.ts:17-22 | THE ANTI-STARVATION MECHANISM IS STRUCTURALLY DEAD. `canStarvationOverride` = `starvationOverrideEligible && !isCriticalPressure() && !STARVATION_OVERRIDE_DISABLED_LANES.has(lane)`. `starvationOverrideEligible` can only become true for a lane with a FINITE `MAX_DEFERRED_FRAMES_BY_LANE` entry — and that map has exactly three keys {selection*feedback, phase_b_materialization, overlay_shell_transition}, ALL THREE of which are members of STARVATION_OVERRIDE_DISABLED_LANES. Every other lane gets POSITIVE_INFINITY and is never eligible. Therefore `canStarvationOverride` is a compile-time-constant FALSE, and the `deferredFrameCount` bookkeeping (L157) feeds nothing. A guard that cannot fail. Failure enabled: a deferred phase_b/overlay task can be starved indefinitely under sustained frame pressure with no escape hatch, while the code reads as though one exists. Ladder: compensates-for = wanting both "heavy lanes must not hog a frame" and "heavy lanes must not starve"; bedrock = those are the SAME set of lanes, so the two policies were written against each other; rederive = pick one owner for heavy-lane admission — either the governor's one-heavy-lane-per-frame rule (which already bounds hogging) OR a deferral cap, not both, and make the cap apply to the lanes it was written for; red-team = the dead override may be load-bearing-by-absence (enabling it could admit a heavy task during critical pressure and regress the very jank the governor was built to kill), so this must be re-derived as a decision, not "fixed" by deleting a set membership. | ESCALATED (owner decision: which policy owns heavy-lane admission) | set-intersection proof read from both files; no test covers the override
F1001 | mobile-search | runtime/scheduler/frame-budget-governor.ts:54,56,64 | SECOND DEAD GUARD IN THE SAME FILE. `canRun` returns false at L56 when `projectedSpentMs > HARD_BUDGET_MS` (12). L64 then returns `projectedSpentMs <= availableBudgetMs` where `availableBudgetMs = TARGET_FRAME_MS - RESERVED_HEADROOM_MS` = 16.67-4 = 12.67. Any value reaching L64 is already <= 12, hence always <= 12.67 — L64 is tautologically TRUE. So `TARGET_FRAME_MS`, `RESERVED_HEADROOM_MS` and `availableBudgetMs` are inert: three constants and a computation whose sole consumer can never be false. Ladder: compensates-for = two budget models (a frame-headroom model and a hard/soft-budget model) landed on top of each other; bedrock = there is ONE budget question ("may this task run in the remaining frame?"); rederive = delete the headroom model and keep hard/soft, or derive HARD_BUDGET_MS FROM `TARGET_FRAME_MS - RESERVED_HEADROOM_MS` so the constants are related by construction instead of by coincidence; red-team = the 12 vs 12.67 gap is only 0.67ms, so behavior is unchanged either way — this is a legibility/honesty fix, not a perf fix, and should not be sold as one. | ESCALATED | arithmetic proof; HEAVY_LANES (governor L17-22) is also a byte-identical duplicate of STARVATION_OVERRIDE_DISABLED_LANES (scheduler L59-64) — one set, two homes
F1002 | mobile-search | runtime/map/map-query-budget.ts (whole file, 168 lines) | A WRITE-ONLY INSTRUMENT ON THE HYDRATION HOT PATH. `MapQueryBudget.snapshot()` has ZERO callers repo-wide (verified: grep for `mapQueryBudget.snapshot`, `MapQueryBudgetSnapshot`, `indexQueryDurationP95`, `runtimeAttributionTopContributors`, `fullCatalogScanCount` across apps/ + packages/ returns nothing outside the file itself). `resetRun()` also has zero callers. Meanwhile `recordRuntimeAttributionDurationMs` IS called on live paths (use-search-results-hydration-key-apply-runtime.ts:20-21, use-search-results-sectioned-projection-telemetry-runtime.ts:49, use-search-results-hydration-rows-release-emission-runtime.ts:35, search-runtime-profiler-attribution-runtime.ts:32). So every results hydration commit pays two Map get/set operations to accumulate numbers nobody ever reads, into totals nobody ever resets. Additionally `recordIndexQueryDurationMs`, `recordReadModelBuildSliceDurationMs`, `recordFullCatalogScan` and `incrementRuntimeCounter` have zero callers, so four snapshot fields (`indexQueryDurationP95`, `readModelBuildSliceP95`, `fullCatalogScanCount`, `runtimeCounters`) are structurally always 0/empty. This is the purest always-green instrument in the territory: it cannot show RED because nothing can observe it. Ladder: compensates-for = a measurement campaign whose reader (a debug surface) was never built or was deleted; bedrock = an instrument exists to be read, and per CLAUDE.md a metric that can only ever show green is lying; rederive = either wire a reader (the search-perf-debug surface) and add a per-probe `resetRun()` so the P95/mean semantics are honest, or delete the class and its five call sites; red-team = the nav-switch attribution instrument two files over (runtime/shared/search-nav-switch-runtime-attribution.ts) is the CORRECT shape — env-gated, bounded ring, zero-cost when off, genuinely readable — so the fix is to converge on that shape rather than invent a third. | ESCALATED (owner decision: wire a reader or delete) | repo-wide grep, zero readers; contrasted against the working instrument
F1003 | mobile-search | runtime/shared/search-runtime-bus.ts:436-458 | THE BUS'S RESULTS-ATTRIBUTION INSTRUMENT IS BLIND TO ITS OWN TARGET CASE. `recordDiagnostic` early-returns at L436 when `durationMs < 0.25 && changedKeys.size <= 1`. The `logPerfScenarioStackAttribution` call for `results`/`resultsRequestKey` writes (L440-458) sits BELOW that filter. A fast single-key `results` publish — the most common results write there is — is therefore never attributed. The noise filter was placed above the signal it was meant to capture. Ladder: compensates-for = ring-buffer noise from trivial publishes; bedrock = "is this publish worth recording in the ring" and "does this publish need stack attribution" are two different questions with two different thresholds; rederive = hoist the `shouldCaptureStack` decision above the noise filter, keep the filter for the ring only; red-team = stack capture is expensive (it builds a stack trace), so hoisting it makes every single-key results publish pay that cost — the honest shape is to gate stack capture on the perf-scenario flag (as logPerfScenarioStackAttribution already does internally) rather than on duration, so the filter and the attribution stop fighting. | ESCALATED | read; the filter predicate and the attribution predicate are independent by construction
F1004 | mobile-search | runtime/shared/search-runtime-bus.ts:356-390 | NO LISTENER ERROR ISOLATION IN THE CENTRAL BUS. `notify()` invokes listeners inside a bare `this.listeners.forEach`. One listener that throws aborts the whole loop: every listener registered after it is silently never notified for that change, the `recordDiagnostic` call at L381 never runs, and the bus's `version` has already been bumped — so the surface is now desynced from a state the bus believes it published. Failure enabled: a single bad subscriber (e.g. a controller reading a torn-down ref) silently freezes an arbitrary subset of the search surface, with no error surfaced and no diagnostic recorded — an intermittent "half the screen stopped updating" with a clean log. Ladder: compensates-for = nothing; this is an omission, not a compensation; bedrock = a fan-out must not let one subscriber's failure determine another subscriber's delivery; rederive = wrap each `listener()` in try/catch, record the throw into the diagnostics ring (so it can show RED), and continue the loop; red-team = swallowing listener errors can hide real bugs, so the catch must RECORD (ring + logger.warn) rather than ignore — a silent catch here would trade one invisible failure for another. Also note Map.forEach visits entries ADDED during iteration, so a listener that subscribes during notify is notified for a change it did not observe from before subscribing (minor, but same loop). | ESCALATED | read; no try/catch anywhere in notify/publish/batch
F1005 | mobile-search | runtime/resolver/search-world-resolver.ts:360-361 | CANCELLATION IS CLASSIFIED BY SNIFFING AN ERROR MESSAGE STRING. `const isCanceled = reason.includes('canceled') || reason.includes('runSearch returned no response');` decides whether a failed resolution raises a user-visible failure level (retry chip + failed empty state) or is treated as expected lifecycle. Failure enabled, both directions: (a) any upstream reword, axios/AbortController message change, or localized message silently reclassifies a normal session-exit cancellation as a real failure — the user gets a failure banner on an ordinary back-navigation; (b) a genuine network error whose message happens to contain the substring "canceled" is silently swallowed and never reported. The comment claims this is "classified HERE, once" — which is true and good — but the classifier itself is the weak link. Ladder: compensates-for = the fetch layer not distinguishing cancellation from failure in its type; bedrock = cancellation is a distinct OUTCOME of a resolution, not a flavour of error text; rederive = have `fetchWorldForTuple` reject with a typed `SearchFetchCanceledError` (or resolve a discriminated `{kind:'canceled'}`), and check `instanceof`/`kind` here; red-team = the second substring ('runSearch returned no response') encodes a REAL second condition (an empty response treated as a cancellation), so a naive `instanceof AbortError` swap would lose it — the typed outcome must cover both cases, which is an argument for a discriminated result rather than an error subclass. | ESCALATED | read; both substrings are load-bearing today
F1006 | mobile-search | runtime/resolver/search-world-resolver.ts:403 | A CURRENCY GUARD THAT CANNOT FAIL, NEXT TO THE REAL ONE. `resolveNextPage` calls `core.land({generation, worldKey: appendKey}, () => true)` — the currency predicate is the literal `true`, and the returned disposition is DISCARDED. The real currency check is then done separately at L404 (`if (isTupleStillDesired(tuple))`). Compare the page-one path at L315-318, where the same `core.land` call passes the real predicate and BRANCHES on the disposition. So the append path has the same question answered twice, once vacuously and once for real, in two different mechanisms. Ladder: compensates-for = wanting `core.land` to always clear the in-flight append key regardless of currency (a legitimate need — the key must be released or load-more wedges forever); bedrock = "release the in-flight key" and "may this result present?" are two responsibilities the resolver core currently fuses into one call; rederive = give the core an explicit `release(key)` separate from `land(key, isCurrent)`, so the append path releases unconditionally and asks the currency question once, in one place; red-team = splitting the core's API touches the page-one path too and the core has a spec (search-world-resolver-core.spec.ts) that encodes the fused semantics — this is a real refactor, not a one-liner, and the current code is CORRECT, just doubly-stated. | ESCALATED (legibility; no live defect) | read + compared against the page-one path at L315

F1008 | mobile-search | runtime/map/camera-intent-arbiter.ts:103-120,207-218 | `allowDuringGesture` IS HONORED BY commit() BUT NOT BY THE COMPLETION TRACKER, SO THE DEFERRED CAMERA-STATE SYNC IS SILENTLY DROPPED. `commit()` admits an intent during a gesture when `allowDuringGesture === true` (L124) and arms `pendingProgrammaticCameraCompletionId` plus, for `deferControlledCameraStateUntilCompletion` callers, `pendingControlledCameraStateSync` (L165-175). But `setGestureActive(true)` unconditionally cancels and CLEARS both (L109-118), and map-interaction-controller.ts:270 calls `setGestureActive(isUserViewportGestureActive)` on EVERY native viewport-changed tick — many times per second during a pan. So an allowDuringGesture intent issued mid-gesture is un-tracked within roughly one frame: its completion is reported as 'cancelled' though the animation was explicitly permitted, and its deferred controlled-camera-state sync (center/zoom/bearing/pitch/padding) NEVER FLUSHES — the JS-side controlled camera state stays stale. Reachable: `allowDuringGesture: true` has six live call sites (profile-native-command-runtime.ts:45, use-search-session-origin-camera-runtime.ts:73, use-search-root-map-bootstrap-runtime.ts:80, use-search-runtime-instrumentation-runtime.ts:174/223/250, navigation/runtime/use-app-route-scene-camera-motion-target-runtime.ts:111). ATTRIBUTION CORRECTION, recorded honestly: my first hypothesis was a PERMANENT wedge (pending id stuck forever, `syncObservedCameraViewport` dead for the session). That was WRONG — I red-teamed it against map-interaction-controller.ts:270/435/575/582 and found the repeated setGestureActive(true) self-heals the stuck-id case within a frame. The surviving defect is narrower and real: the dropped deferred sync + a lying 'cancelled' status. Ladder: compensates-for = wanting a user gesture to always win over a programmatic camera; bedrock = `allowDuringGesture` is the caller declaring THIS intent coexists with the gesture, and that declaration must be carried through the whole lifecycle, not just the admission check; rederive = store `allowDuringGesture` on the pending record and have setGestureActive skip cancellation for pendings that carry it; red-team = the gestureActive clause in `consumeProgrammaticCameraCompletion` (L210) is ALSO redundant for its stated purpose — setGestureActive(true) already clears the pending id, so the id-match at L211 alone rejects stale completions — meaning the safest minimal change is to drop L210's gestureActive clause and gate the cancellation, but this is the finished-map's JS seam and must be validated by eye (a profile-open camera move overlapping a pan) before any edit. | ESCALATED (owner validation required — JS map seam) | read + reachability grep + red-teamed against the gesture controller
F1009 | mobile-search | runtime/map/camera-intent-arbiter.ts:177-189 vs 191-203 | TRUTH IN TWO PLACES: the camera writer sequence is duplicated verbatim. Lines 177-189 (the `commandCameraViewport` succeeded, non-deferred arm) and 191-203 (the fell-through arm) are byte-identical 13-line blocks: setMapCameraAnimation → padding sync → setMapCenter → setMapZoom → conditional bearing → conditional pitch. A third near-copy of the same sequence lives at L232-247 (`flushControlledCameraStateSync`). Any change to write ORDER (which matters — animation mode must be set before center/zoom or the move animates when it should snap) must be made in two-to-three places. F1011 | mobile-search | runtime/shared/search-runtime-bus-context.ts (whole file) | A DEAD SECOND REACT CONTEXT FOR THE SAME BUS — dead code that is also a LANDMINE. This file creates its own `React.createContext<SearchRuntimeBus|null>(null)` and exports `SearchRuntimeBusProvider` + `useSearchRuntimeBus`. The LIVE context is a different object created in search-runtime-bus.ts:495 (`SearchRuntimeBusContext`) with its own `useSearchBus`. Ladder: compensates-for = an extraction of the context out of the bus module that was started and abandoned; bedrock = one bus, one context, one provider, one hook; rederive = delete this file (the live context already lives with the bus and the co-location is fine); red-team = the extraction instinct was not wrong — a context in its own module avoids importing the whole bus class for a type-only consumer — but if that is wanted, MOVE the live one rather than leaving a decoy, and delete either way. F1012 | mobile-search | runtime/shared/ (structural) | THE CENTRAL ARCHITECTURAL FACT OF THIS TERRITORY, measured rather than asserted: runtime/shared holds 534 non-spec files with a MEDIAN of 55 lines (p25=30, p75=91, min=5, max=1475); 187 files are under 40 lines; 67 export no runtime value at all (type-only). 324 of 534 (61%) have exactly ONE consumer in the entire repo, and 42 have none (F1010). Three independent full-read passes over disjoint sub-clusters converged on the same verdict from different evidence: 48 of 61 files in the overlay cluster, 35 of 176 in the search-root cluster, and ~6 in the results-presentation cluster are PURE PASS-THROUGHS — a hook whose body forwards its arguments to one child hook, spreads two children with no logic, or wraps a single already-stable value in a useMemo. Named families: `*-args.ts`(foreground-editing, 7 files, a four-level spread tree producing one flat object),`_-ports.ts`(submit, 13 files, of which 3 are load-bearing),`_-patch-runtime.ts`(hydration, 8 files, four levels deep to assemble a six-field object, one leaf existing solely to return the literal`false`), `_-lane.ts`/`_-control-lanes.ts`(11 single-field useMemo wrappers across 3 files). Ladder: compensates-for = a per-file size budget applied to composition code, plus a belief that each seam is a memoization boundary; bedrock = a seam earns its file only if it owns something — state, a lifetime, a subscription, or a memo the caller could not already have; rederive = keep the seams that own LIFETIME (the`_-authority-runtime`family, the map ref-latch bridges, the stage boundaries — these are genuinely excellent) and collapse the four ceremonial families, removing roughly 30-40 files and ~700-1000 lines without moving a single behavioral decision; red-team = THIS IS THE MOST DANGEROUS PROPOSAL IN THE PASS and must not be run as a mechanical sweep. Three concrete reasons: (a) the decomposition is load-bearing for the CLAUDE.md law that scene body-spec hooks never fire effects — collapsing a body-spec hook into a committed one, or vice versa, silently changes whether effects run, and the boundary is not visible from the file name; (b) the pass-through hooks preserve hook ORDER, which F1013 shows is the real wiring contract — merging two hooks changes call order; (c) at least one ceremonial family actively CONCEALED a live defect (the hydration patch chain hid a missing subscription), which argues the collapse is valuable but also that these files are load-bearing as the place bugs currently hide, so the collapse must be done cluster-by-cluster with tsc+jest green at each step, not repo-wide. | ESCALATED (owner decision: appetite + sequencing) | measured (wc/grep census) + three independent full-read passes
F1013 | mobile-search | runtime/shared/use-search-root-runtime-stage-runtime.ts:36-217, use-search-root-runtime-control-stage-runtime.ts:61-231, use-search-root-control-foundation-authority-runtime.ts:48-75 | CALLER-MUST-REMEMBER: THE WIRING IS THE HOOK CALL ORDER, AND NOTHING ENFORCES IT. Fourteen stages in the top orchestrator and twelve in the control stage consume each other by field path;`clearRestoreAuthorityRuntime`must be called LAST because it takes the other four authorities as inputs. A future edit that hoists one hook call — a natural "group the authorities together" tidy — produces`undefined` reads four levels deep (`controlAuthorityRuntime.foundationAuthorityRuntime.profileBridgeAuthorityRuntime.profileBridge`) that TypeScript cannot catch, because every intermediate is typed non-optional. Ladder: compensates-for = React's linear-hook-order requirement, which is a REAL constraint, not an invented one; bedrock = dataflow is a DAG and hook order is one valid topological sort of it; rederive = there is nothing better to build here — this IS the honest encoding of a DAG in hooks, and inverting it into a context/provider tree would hide the order behind indirection while adding render layers, which is strictly worse; the cheap and correct win is a one-line ordering comment at each of the three stage boundaries naming what depends on what, so the constraint is documented rather than archaeological. | IDEAL-VERIFIED (shape is right; document the constraint) | read all three stage files end-to-end

This file is what "rederived" looks like when it is done. | IDEAL-VERIFIED | full read

F964 | mobile-nav-overlays | apps/mobile/src/overlays/sheetUtils.ts:65-88 vs apps/mobile/src/navigation/runtime/AppRouteSceneChromeMotionRuntimeProvider.tsx:55-68 | THE SNAP GEOMETRY IS COMPUTED IN TWO PLACES WITH THE SAME UNEXPLAINED LITERALS. `calculateSnapPoints` (the shared sheet snap resolver, 12 consumers) computes `rawMiddle = screenHeight * 0.4`, `middle = Math.max(expanded + 96, rawMiddle)`, `hidden = screenHeight + 80`, `clampedMiddle = Math.min(middle, hidden - 120)`. The chrome-motion provider's `buildExpandedMiddleChromeSnaps` (F949) recomputes the IDENTICAL four expressions with the IDENTICAL four literals. Neither file explains any of them, and neither imports the other. So the sheet and its chrome derive their geometry independently from a copied formula — the classic setup where tuning one drifts the other silently. `hidden - 120` is `screenHeight - 40` written the long way in both. Additional unexplained literals local to `calculateSnapPoints`: `resolvedHeaderHeight = headerHeight > 0 ? headerHeight : 96` (a magic fallback height for a value the repo now computes exactly — see `computeSceneChromeHeight`, F957 — so the fallback should be the computed height, not 96) and `finalCollapsed = Math.max(navAlignedCollapsed, clampedMiddle + 24)`. Ladder: bedrock = one snap-geometry function; rederive = the chrome provider imports `calculateSnapPoints` (it already receives the same inputs), and every literal becomes a named constant with a stated derivation, the way overlay-chrome-metrics.ts already does for the chrome row (F966). | ESCALATED (design; merges with F948/F949) | read both

Also note two springs coexist with no stated relationship: `SHEET_SPRING_CONFIG` here is `{damping: 28, stiffness: 320, overshootClamping: false}` while `DEFAULT_TRANSITION_SPRING_CONFIG` (transition-lane-player.ts:38) is `{damping: 28, stiffness: 220, overshootClamping: true}` — the second documents its near-critical derivation, the first documents nothing. | ESCALATED (deletion + one comment) | grep verified per symbol

(1) `overlay-chrome-metrics.ts`: every chrome-row token is a named declared constant and `OVERLAY_TAB_HEADER_HEIGHT` is the literal SUM of them — so the height is a derivation, not a measurement or a guess. The header states why it is RN-free (so the pure geometry module and its jest contracts can consume it) and why the sum is physically true ("OverlaySheetHeaderChrome renders `fixedHeight` — text conforms to the box, never the reverse"). This is exactly what F949/F964's literals are missing. (2) `sheetBodyScrollDefaults.ts`: 30 lines of comment explaining the PHYSICS of the down-handoff, why three separate bounce flags are needed (iOS needs both `bounces` and `alwaysBounceVertical`; Android needs `overScrollMode`), that it is applied STRUCTURALLY after the prop spread so it cannot be overridden per scene, and — best of all — it records the alternative that was tried and REVERTED (a direction-gated bounce) with the two defects that killed it. A doc comment that names the road not taken is worth more than one that only describes the road taken. (3) `scene-chrome-ack-runtime.ts`: a module-scope store with ONE named writer, an explicit test seam, and a closing paragraph declaring what is now DEAD and unrepresentable (the measured-chrome-height cache) so nobody re-adds it. | - | read

F977 | mobile-nav-overlays | apps/mobile/src/overlays/OverlaySheetHeaderChrome.tsx:32-45 + PersistentSheetHeaderHost.tsx:98-115,196-206,298 | THE FLAG THAT CANNOT FLIP, AND IT DISARMS THE L1 INSTRUMENT. `OverlaySheetHeaderChrome` declares nine props (`grabHandleCutout, fixedHeight, paddingTop, paddingHorizontal, transparent, afterRow, rowStyle, style, onLayout`); its SOLE render site (PersistentSheetHeaderHost.tsx:298) passes NONE of them. So `fixedHeight` is permanently `true`, which makes `measuredHeight` state (:98), the `setMeasuredHeight` branch (:112-115) and the `(measuredHeight ?? OVERLAY_TAB_HEADER_HEIGHT)` arm (:100-102) unreachable; and `grabHandleCutout` is permanently `true`, so both `: null` arms (:204, :212) are dead. The consequence is the interesting part: the `[CHROME-GEOMETRY]` bark at :196-206 (`if (Math.abs(computed - measured) > 0.5) console.error('a chrome constant is stale')`) compares `computeSceneChromeHeight` — a SUM of the `overlay-chrome-metrics` constants — against a measurement of a box whose height IS `OVERLAY_TAB_HEADER_HEIGHT`, the same sum, because `fixedHeight` is true. Change any metric constant and BOTH sides move together. The instrument can only fire for a strip-height mismatch or an unmanaged-height citizen — far narrower than the "a chrome constant is stale" claim it prints. This is the RED counterpart the L1 design leans on, and as wired it is green by construction. Ladder: rederive = delete the eight unused props (which makes the always-true branches unrepresentable), then re-aim the instrument at the thing it can actually falsify, and say so in its message. | ESCALATED (design; this is an always-green instrument guarding a law) | read

Nine hand-written comparators across this scope (`shouldSkipSceneStackBodyContentLayerUpdate`, `shouldSkipSceneStackBodyContentUpdate`, `areSceneBodySurfaceSelectionsEqual`, `areSearchRouteSceneBodyContentSpecsEqual`, `areSearchRouteSceneBodyTransportSpecsEqual`, plus five in the host) each enumerate 10-20 fields BY HAND. This is the single highest-leverage structural fix in the overlays root. | ESCALATED (design) | read

F983 | mobile-nav-overlays | apps/mobile/src/overlays/BottomSheetSceneStackListBodySurface.tsx:255-302 + SearchMountedSceneBody.tsx:796-855 | THE MVCP LAW HAS NO STRUCTURAL ENFORCEMENT AT THE TRANSPORT MERGE SITES. `grep -rn "maintainVisibleContentPosition" apps/mobile/src/overlays` (excluding panels/) returns NOTHING — the sheet machinery never sets it, so every FlashList it renders runs FlashList 2.0.2's default (ON). The re-sortable feeds that must disable it do so via per-scene `flashListProps` from panels/. The risk: that per-scene `{ maintainVisibleContentPosition: { disabled: true } }` is threaded through FOUR spread/merge sites, and at least one of them spreads scene props in an order where a later object can overwrite an earlier one (`sceneSecondaryFlashListProps` at BottomSheetSceneStackListBodySurface.tsx:275 spreads `sceneSecondaryInputFlashListProps` AFTER the defaults; the dead BottomSheetWithFlashList.tsx:239-246 does the same with `activeFlashListProps` after `secondaryList.flashListProps`). There is no sweep and no type-level guard making "disabled on a re-sortable list" structural — it is a per-scene opt-in that the transport can silently drop. Given CLAUDE.md records this as a hard-won law with a specific expensive symptom, it deserves better than a spread order. Ladder: rederive = the transport owns the default (disabled), and a scene must explicitly OPT IN to MVCP (append/chat lists), inverting the failure mode from silent-breakage to explicit-declaration. | ESCALATED (design) | grep verified

F984 | mobile-nav-overlays | apps/mobile/src/overlays/bottomSheetSurfaceStyleUtils.ts + page-body-contract.ts + ChromeTitleText.tsx:21-24 + useBottomSheetSharedGestureRuntime.ts (the mount-stable pan memo) + BottomSheetSceneStackBodyActivityContext.ts | IDEAL-VERIFIED, five more, argued. (1) `bottomSheetSurfaceStyleUtils.ts`: `SceneBodyContentInsets` permits exactly padding + `backgroundColor`, replacing `sanitizeContentContainerStyle`, which SILENTLY STRIPPED anything else (a `flex` sent through the transport just vanished — the W4 dmSession regression). Silent-strip converted into a compile error at the producer is the single highest-leverage move available in this codebase, and it was made here. (2) `page-body-contract.ts`: a closed, total five-state enum where the query lives in the controller and row components receive RESOLVED items — so a row component cannot express a pending branch because the pending case never renders it. With one interpreter (`PageBodyShell`) and one failure chokepoint, "a page-local retry view has nowhere to exist". Its spec asserts totality directly. That is the difference between a rule and an architecture. (3) `ChromeTitleText`'s `SingleLineText` brand makes a multi-line chrome title unrepresentable AT THE TYPE LEVEL, not merely clipped at render — the correct answer to the same class as F980's memo landmine, and worth citing as the target shape. (4) The gesture runtime's `React.useMemo(..., [])` pan minting with its standing order ("every capture is a stable SharedValue, a module constant, or a worklet defined inside this memo"), SharedValue mirrors in and a seq-counter command bus out: a genuinely hard concurrency problem (RNGH binds a gesture to one detector; relations are declared native-side) solved by making the bad state unrepresentable, with the symptom AND the mechanism recorded so nobody "cleans up" the empty dep array. (5) `BottomSheetSceneStackBodyActivityContext`'s volatility split — separating the transition-STABLE object from a primitive-valued `isActive` context, because bundling them handed every consumer a new context object per transition and re-rendered whole resident bodies (~75ms, measured, `[COMMITDBG]`-attributed). The attribution rides in the comment, so nobody re-merges them. | - | read
